#5217. Binance Drained of $5.5 Billion in Suspected Hack, Tether and Circle Freeze $1.8 Billion

Binance Drained of $5.5 Billion in Suspected Hack, Tether and Circle Freeze $1.8 Billion

At least $5.57 billion in crypto has left wallets linked to Binance across three blockchains in what appears to be a breach of the world’s largest cryptocurrency exchange.

If confirmed as a hack, it would be the biggest crypto hack on record. Binance has not confirmed its exchange has been hacked but said it is aware of the “potential security issue and is actively investigating.”

“We have not confirmed any impact to user funds,” the company posted on X. “Our security teams are reviewing all relevant information with urgency, and we will provide updates through official Binance channels as soon as we have verified information.”

Our teams are aware of reports of a potential security issue and are actively investigating. At this stage, we have not confirmed any impact to user funds.

Our security teams are reviewing all relevant information with urgency, and we will provide updates through official… pic.twitter.com/dN76yhzYM0

Binance is the world's largest crypto exchange, where millions of people buy, sell and store their coins. The money came from its hot wallets, the internet-connected wallets exchanges use to process customer withdrawals.

The tally counts $3.51 billion on Ethereum, $1.44 billion in 18,270 Bitcoin and $630 million on Tron. The first Ethereum transaction, at 20:24 UTC, sent 167,464 ETH plus 63 token transfers to a brand-new address, including 1.615 billion USDT, a stablecoin built to always trade at $1, according to Etherscan.

Three minutes earlier, an address funded through Tornado Cash, a mixer that scrambles the trail of crypto payments, submitted a delegation for a Binance hot wallet using EIP-7702, an Ethereum feature that lets an ordinary wallet run smart-contract code. “7702 lets a normal wallet execute delegated code. The delegation persists; that code can authorize later transfers without another signature from the wallet,” Odysseas Lamtzidi, founder of Phylax Systems wrote on X.

1/4 EIP-7702 can turn the wallet itself into the sweeper.

A Binance-labeled wallet moved 167,464 ETH + 63 tokens, including 1.615B USDT, just 3m24s after a signed delegation was installed.

This is bad.https://t.co/GrvUiya16D pic.twitter.com/zONu8cwW63

— Odysseus | phylax.systems (@odysseas_eth) October 29, 2026

How the attacker got the required signature is still unknown. Tether froze 975.1 million USDT in the main receiving address, and Circle, issuer of the rival stablecoin USDC, froze 823 million USDC in the same wallet at 21:04 UTC, bringing the total frozen to about $1.8 billion. Both issuers can block specific addresses from moving their tokens, but the attacker is moving funds fast.

"Circle is closely tracking reports of a potential exploit involving Binance," Circle CEO Jeremy Allaire posted on X. "In coordination with law enforcement, we have frozen more than 800M USDC associated with the incident and will continue supporting the investigation."

Total on the hack is at least $5.57B across 3 chains (likely more)

Tether has started freezing and this time even Circle acted and managed to freeze 800M in a single wallet.

Still $800M could be frozen, attacker is moving funds fast https://t.co/km4eyJWEa8 pic.twitter.com/IL6wBfZK2r

Binance lost 7,000 Bitcoin, about $40 million, in a 2019 hack. The $5.57 billion tally would be more than triple the roughly $1.5 billion Bybit heist in February 2025, the previous record.

#5216. Vitalik is making private Ethereum payments without a crypto website

Retaining local code can reduce repeated website delivery while leaving package updates, signatures and submission as separate trust decisions.

His objection goes beyond screen clutter. A website can deliver application code again whenever someone returns, leaving the user dependent on what the server supplies each time. Keeping a local application can reduce that repeated delivery exposure. It also moves a consequential decision onto the user: which software revision to install, retain and eventually replace.

The wallet he linked makes the trade-off concrete. Its documentation warns that the CLI is unaudited, under active development and written with AI assistance. It tells users to avoid mainnet unless they understand what they are doing and the possibility of catastrophic fund loss.

Control over a downloaded interface matters because the accepted code sits between a payment instruction and the signature authorizing it. Choosing when that code changes can reduce one recurring exposure, while its dependencies and signing access determine what the installed software can do.

The practical gain is the ability to keep a chosen application revision rather than obtaining the website interface again on each visit. Accepting a replacement revision creates another delivery decision. For this source-build route, that decision also involves the dependency set and the tools that turn source into executable software. A familiar project name alone does not describe all the code being accepted.

The documented installation route also shows what “local” entails. The project's instructions call for installing dependencies with npm install, building the application with npm run build, and running a local launcher. At commit 0ef5c44, the documentation, manifest, lockfile and implementation describe the same application revision. Its manifest identifies version 0.1.0 and requires Node.js 22 or later. Buterin's account does not identify his installed revision.

The manifest fixes direct dependency versions, including viem and several Kohaku packages. It also pins a separate kohaku-artifacts Git dependency to a commit. The corresponding lockfile records resolved registry packages and integrity hashes. The application therefore has a software supply chain even when its interface runs on the user's machine.

Kohaku-cli's public-transfer and Railgun unshield paths show why a pause before submission can mean different things. An ordinary public-transfer dry run returns without sending the transaction; a prepared Railgun unshield can already contain a signature.

For an ordinary, non-batched public transfer, the transfer implementation simulates the transaction and returns dry-run output without submission. The broadcast path requires the sender's private key, constructs a local wallet client and sends the transaction. The wallet-client code derives its signing account from that private key.

Private operations expose an additional wrinkle. The unshield command can output a prepared Railgun UserOperation that is already signed, even when broadcasting is withheld. A UserOperation is an account-operation payload that compatible tooling can submit. The separate broadcast implementation expects a signed operation and then sends it through a Pimlico bundler.

For that path, withholding submission can still leave a signed operation ready for compatible tooling to submit later. Calling the result “prepared” therefore does not make it universally unsigned. Software that may construct an authorization, software that may sign it and software that may submit it need to be assessed as distinct sources of authority, even when one CLI coordinates them.

Human confirmation is another separate choice. In the inspected public-transfer path, a suitably configured caller with wallet access can use --non-interactive together with --broadcast to submit without a further confirmation prompt. Wallet unlocking, required parameters, chain matching and simulation checks still apply. The capability is relevant to scripts and agents; it does not reveal whether Buterin uses unattended payments.

The README describes an encrypted seed on disk and owner-restricted password files for POSIX automation. Those measures govern stored secrets and how a process obtains access to them. They do not, by themselves, establish an independent spending limit or recipient policy once the process can use the signer.

CryptoSlate has previously covered the wallet-permission problem for local AI. Delivery control adds another dimension: a user can retain a chosen interface revision, while that code operates within whatever signing and submission authority it receives.

Software provenance offers a way to examine the delivery side more rigorously. It records where an artifact came from and how it was built. The SLSA verification standard describes checking a provenance signature, matching the artifact's digest, recognizing a trusted builder and comparing the stated source and build parameters with expected values.

These checks can help distinguish an expected build from a substituted artifact or an unintended build source. They still depend on which builders and source expectations the verifier trusts.

Dependency checking remains its own task. SLSA describes recursive verification of resolved dependencies as an optional additional step. Authenticating the top-level artifact does not automatically authenticate every input to it.

Correct origin and build history can accompany application logic that the user would still reject.

Buterin's experiment points to a concrete form of user control: choosing the application code that stays on the machine and deciding when to accept replacement code. Its value depends on carrying that control through dependency selection and the signer.

#5215. 6.7 Million Stellar (XLM) Stolen in Massive $40 Million Mercado Bitcoin Breach

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

Brazilian crypto platform Mercado Bitcoin has confirmed the detection and containment of an issue in one of its systems. As a precaution, the exchange has temporarily suspended deposits and withdrawals, assuring clients of the safety of their assets and the absence of a data leak.

The incident occurred shortly after the platform passed a local licensing deadline.

According to independent blockchain company Hypernative, suspicious outflows from wallets linked to the exchange exceeded $40 million (approximately 217 million reais). Analysts recorded the movement of assets on the Ethereum, Bitcoin, Solana, Tron and Stellar (XLM) networks.

In total, the activity spanned at least 10 blockchains, also including Polygon, Arbitrum, Avalanche and BNB Smart Chain. The first suspicious transfers were recorded at around 02:15 UTC and involved Bitcoin deposit addresses attributed to the exchange.

Among the flagged assets were 106 BTC and 2,852 ETH. The hackers carried out direct token transfers, which indicates a compromise of operational reserves rather than segregated client assets.

Among the withdrawn assets, blockchain analysts recorded a large volume of Stellar tokens: about 6.7 million XLM were consolidated at addresses linked to the suspicious transactions.

As part of the investigation and coordination of efforts with the authorities, Mercado Bitcoin published a specific Stellar destination address to help the community and security firms track the movement of assets in real time.

The move is aimed at monitoring the movement of tokens and preventing their laundering through third-party services.

At the moment, the exchange is conducting an investigation, tracking the assets and promising to provide regular updates. The exchange has not yet confirmed the amount involved, disclosed which system was compromised or given a date for resuming deposits and withdrawals.

Until the platform fully resumes normal operations and the security of its infrastructure is confirmed, users are advised to refrain from making new deposits.

#5214. Pando Rings hacker moves 4,000 ETH through Tornado Cash after 4 years – Details

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Four years after the 2022 Pando Rings exploit, the attacker is once again using Tornado Cash to launder the stolen funds. The wallet first converted approximately $16.98 million in Dai [DAI] to 7,548 Ethereum [ETH] and began dividing the assets into multiple small transfers.

Multiple transactions were found showing a series of 100 ETH deposits, each worth approximately $229,000.

To move their funds, the attacker initially used Aave [AAVE] to convert stablecoin holdings to ETH before sending them through Tornado Cash. At the end of October, the attacker supplied 16.8 million USDT as collateral to borrow 5,000 ETH and repay the loan.

After repaying the loan, the attacker withdrew the collateral and exchanged 16.7 million DAI for 7,548 ETH. These steps show how the attacker consolidated funds by lending and swapping tokens before laundering them.

Thereafter, the attacker made a partial deposit of 4,000 ETH to Tornado Cash and left 3,548 ETH outside the mix. In doing so, this transfer complicated both the recovery of the funds and obscured the eventual destination of the funds.

However, it did not establish whether the funds were immediately sold or if there was widespread selling pressure on exchanges.

As the exploiter continues to break up and hide the trail of these funds, he also increases the chances of distributing additional funds and complicating recovery efforts. Still, the deposits themselves do not establish that the exploiter has either sold his ETH or transferred it to exchanges.

Tornado Cash complicates the recovery process. This is because it removes the link between a user and other parties in a transaction. For example, when multiple users place their funds into Tornado Cash, the funds enter a shared pool.

Once placed in the pool, funds may be returned to users through a new wallet address. As such, investigators can rely less heavily on tracking wallets directly related to the theft.

This creates another challenge for investigators, who must rely on transaction patterns and off-chain intelligence to identify where the ETH eventually moves. The exploiter’s earlier 800 ETH deposit in August also shows that this approach predates the latest transfers.

However, the current deposits have yet to generate any connections to wallets associated with known clusters. Until some of these funds appear at identifiable exchange addresses, investigators have little visibility into what is happening. Immediate selling pressure also remains unconfirmed.

Ultimately, the Pando Rings exploit remains an active recovery challenge. Continued laundering activity obscures fund movements. However, there are still no confirmed exchange deposits.

The Pando Rings exploiter has resumed laundering funds four years after the attack, moving 4,000 ETH through Tornado Cash.

The renewed activity complicates fund recovery, while 3,548 ETH remain outside the mixer, and no exchange deposits confirm immediate selling.

#5213. XRP’s Security Fix Puts Validator Trust Under Scrutiny

Home » Trendsetter News » XRP’s Security Fix Puts Validator Trust Under Scrutiny

An XRP Ledger security patch released before full code disclosure has sparked debate over vulnerability management and validator influence.

A security patch released before its full code was made public is at the center of a dispute over XRP Ledger governance. In a video addressing the controversy, Kamilah Stevenson, a finance commentator and longtime XRP holder, argues that the restricted disclosure protected the network rather than substantiated allegations of fraud.

The criticism came from Cyber Capital fund manager Justin Bonds, who, according to Stevenson, challenged both the upgrade process and the influence of recommended validator lists. The dispute raises two distinct questions for investors: how developers handle dangerous vulnerabilities, and how much discretion network operators retain.

Stevenson says a researcher reported an overflow vulnerability through the XRP Ledger’s bug bounty program on September 22. The flaw, which she says had existed since around 2015, potentially allowed an attacker to obtain XRP improperly or create unauthorized tokens.

According to her account, developers released version 3.4.1 on September 25 without immediately publishing the full fix code. More than 80% of main validators upgraded that day, and an amendment activated on October 9, leaving nodes without the update unable to remain synchronized.

Dr. Stevenson reports that a subsequent ledger audit found no exploitation or unauthorized XRP creation. The transcript does not identify the auditor or provide the underlying report.

She compares the response with Bitcoin Core’s handling of a 2018 vulnerability: developers initially disclosed a crash risk, withholding its inflation implications until users had time to upgrade.

“You protect the network first and you explain second,” she says. That comparison supports delayed vulnerability disclosure, though it does not by itself resolve concerns about inspecting the XRP update.

Stevenson acknowledges that publishers of recommended Unique Node Lists wield influence because many operators adopt those defaults. Operators can choose their own lists, she says, while Ripple runs only one validator on the most commonly used list.

She also points to the amendment threshold: more than 80% validator support sustained for two weeks. Those safeguards challenge the idea that Ripple can unilaterally rewrite ledger rules, but validator ownership alone does not settle questions about influence over defaults.

In a nutshell, the promised code release and technical write-up would offer firmer grounds for judging this episode than either a fraud allegation or assurances from an XRP holder.

#5212. Strategy’s deployable cash falls 45% as buybacks outpace Bitcoin purchases

Strategy bought 811 BTC for $66 million and spent $208 million on STRC buybacks, while a separate $4.79 billion reserve backed dividend and debt-interest payments.

Strategy‘s deployable USD Cash fell 45.3% during the week ended Nov. 1, from $242 million to $133 million, as preferred-stock buybacks exceeded its Bitcoin purchases.

That balance is available for broader treasury uses. Strategy also held a separate $4.79 billion USD Reserve intended for preferred-stock dividends and debt interest.

The company bought 811 Bitcoin between Oct. 26 and Nov. 1 while setting another record for STRC preferred-stock buybacks.

The company spent approximately $66 million acquiring the cryptocurrency at an average price of $80,846 between Oct. 26 and Nov. 1, according to its Nov. 2 regulatory filing.

The purchase increased Strategy's holdings to 849,327 BTC, acquired for an aggregate $64 billion at an average price of $75,451 per coin.

The acquisition marked a return to larger Bitcoin purchases after Michael Saylor's firm suspended buying for a week while concentrating its spending on preferred-stock repurchases.

Strategy had acquired just 334 BTC, 250 BTC and 267 BTC in the first three reporting weeks of October, respectively. Its latest purchase was the largest since Sept. 28, when the company disclosed acquiring 1,665 BTC for approximately $143 million.

Strategy sold approximately 1,178,552 shares of its MSTR common stock through its at-the-market offering program, generating $164 million in net proceeds.

However, only $66 million, or about 40%, went toward purchasing Bitcoin. The remaining $99 million was allocated to the company's deployable USD Cash balance, which has come under pressure from its increasingly aggressive preferred-stock repurchase program.

The company did not issue any new preferred shares during the reporting period, leaving common-stock sales as its only source of fresh equity capital.

While Strategy resumed accumulating Bitcoin, its spending on STRC continued to accelerate.

The company repurchased approximately 2,092,035 STRC shares for $208 million, surpassing the previous weekly record of $205 million established in late October.

The latest transaction means Strategy spent more than three times as much repurchasing preferred shares as it did acquiring Bitcoin.

It also paid an average of approximately $99.52 per STRC share, bringing the transaction price within 0.5% of the security's $100 stated value. That compares with an average purchase price of approximately $99.06 the previous week.

STRC is a variable-rate perpetual preferred security carrying a 12% annualized dividend rate. Strategy has been purchasing the shares to support their trading price near $100 while reducing the amount of preferred capital outstanding.

Strategy entered the reporting period with approximately $242 million in USD Cash. Despite allocating another $99 million from common-stock sales to that balance, it finished the week with just $133 million after spending $208 million on STRC repurchases.

That represents a $110 million, or 45.3%, weekly decline in deployable cash.

The separate USD Reserve declined from approximately $4.83 billion to $4.79 billion after the company used $43 million to pay preferred-stock dividends during the reporting week. The reserve remains substantially larger than its flexible cash balance but serves a different purpose under the company's capital management framework.

Strategy has spent more than $2 billion on preferred-stock repurchases since it launched the program in July.

The company has about $749 million in remaining authorization under its expanded $3 billion digital credit securities repurchase program, along with an unused $1 billion authorization to repurchase MSTR common shares.

#5211. Ripple Partner Mercado Suspends Withdrawals After Confirming 'Security Incident'

Leading Brazilian cryptocurrency exchange and Ripple blockchain payments partner Mercado Bitcoin has announced a temporary freeze on cryptocurrency deposits and withdrawals after acknowledging a security breach.

As the firm revealed in an official announcement, the security breach did not cause any harm to its users or compromise their personal data.

“We detected and quickly mitigated a security issue in one of our systems this morning,” Mercado Bitcoin said.

Mercado Bitcoin stressed that the incident did not harm any customers or lead to a data breach.

Nevertheless, as a precautionary measure, the firm decided to temporarily halt deposits and withdrawals while its security teams conduct further investigations into the issue and try to locate any assets that could have been affected.

Additionally, Mercado Bitcoin said that it is working with the appropriate authorities to investigate the incident.

In the latest update, Mercado Bitcoin revealed that it continues to work on restoring its operations to normal.

“We are still working to safely restore operations and will keep our customers updated on our official channels,” the exchange announced.

Interestingly, the company also released some cryptocurrency wallet addresses related to suspicious transactions linked to the incident.

The move aims to assist blockchain investigators and other industry players in keeping track of possibly compromised assets on different networks.

According to preliminary analysis shared by blockchain security firm Hypernative, the incident allegedly involved more than $40 million in suspicious cryptocurrency transactions across various blockchain networks.

The list includes Bitcoin (BTC), Ethereum (ETH), Solana (SOL), Tron (TRX), Polygon, Arbitrum, Avalanche, BNB Smart Chain and Stellar (XLM).

Meanwhile, Tether CEO Paolo Ardoino revealed that more than 4.5 million USDT tied to the security incident were frozen by the stablecoin issuer.

Mercado Bitcoin is known for having a strong partnership with San Francisco-based blockchain firm Ripple.

In October 2024, Ripple announced that Mercado Bitcoin, the Brazilian cryptocurrency exchange, would become the first customer in Brazil to utilize its managed end-to-end payments solution.

The partnership aimed to enable cross-border transactions and optimize treasury operations between Brazil and Portugal.

Apart from that, Ripple also revealed that the two firms planned to extend the service to enable international payments for corporate and retail users of Mercado Bitcoin.

#5210. Binance Hacker Hunts for Exit Ramps as DeFi Exchanges Pause Markets

Binance Hacker Hunts for Exit Ramps as DeFi Exchanges Pause Markets

The attacker behind the wallets draining Binance is using decentralized exchanges such as Uniswap and Aave as exits, swapping stablecoins that issuers can freeze for Ethereum that nobody can, according to Etherscan records for 10 wallets, eight of them labeled as exploiters.

Earlier today, wallets linked to the world’s largest crypto exchange were drained of at least $5.5 billion, with the total tally still climbing. The company said it was investigating the security incident but did not confirm user funds were affected, though security experts widely believe the exchange was indeed hacked. It stands to be the largest hack in the crypto industry's 18-year history.

Stablecoin issuers Tether and Circle moved quickly to blacklist the suspected attacker’s addresses in order to freeze a portion of the funds and make them inaccessible. As centralized issuers, they each have the ability to blacklist addresses so their tokens cannot move, and more than $2 billion of those two tokens have been frozen so far by different stablecoin providers. ETH has no issuer, so every dollar converted before a freeze lands is out of their reach.

The swaps run through Uniswap, a decentralized exchange where trades settle automatically with no company in the middle.

One relay wallet received 20,940 ETH ($45.58 Million) from Uniswap’s UniswapX contract at 20:34 UTC, another got 3,552 ETH (over $8 million) at 20:47, and two later wallets took in about 18,085 ETH (around $41 million) from Uniswap contracts, in 7,444 ETH and 10,642 ETH batches, and about 16,498 ETH ($37.5 million), in 6,946 ETH and 9,552 ETH batches.

Aave, a lending app where users deposit crypto as collateral to borrow other coins, offered a second exit. Between 20:57 and 21:01 UTC, one wallet deposited $10 million and then $50 million in USDT and borrowed 3,271 ETH ($7.43 million) and then 16,491 ETH ($37.47 million) against it, which prompted cybersecurity experts Odysseas Lamtzidi, founder of Phylax Systems, to warn of “contagion in full effect.”

Contagion in full effect https://t.co/aNz2snt8E6

— Odysseus | phylax.systems (@odysseas_eth) October 29, 2026

Aave has since frozen its V3 markets on Ethereum mainnet, which blocks new deposits and borrowing.

Kamino, a decentralized finance protocol built on the Solana blockchain, also paused its Solana and Bitcoin markets as a precautionary measure.

Kamino can confirm that it is currently unaffected by the ongoing Binance situation

Out of an abundance of caution, we have temporarily paused borrowing in the SOL/BTC Market

We are actively monitoring the situation and are in communication with various partners. Any further…

The ETH is piling up rather than moving on. A wallet labeled Exploiter 8 still held 195,460 ETH ($444.2 million) at 22:03 UTC, and another 57,891.77 ETH ($131.6 million) reached one labeled Exploiter 11, together about 253,000 ETH, or roughly $570 million. That second pile arrived after four hops in 28 minutes, from Exploiter 4 to 5, 5 to 6, 6 to 7 and 7 to 11.

The attacker also swapped 36 million RLUSD, Ripple’s dollar-backed stablecoin, for 15,439 ETH, while 52.54 million RLUSD remained unfrozen at the holding wallet, according to an on-chain update circulating on X. Etherscan showed the main receiving wallet holding about $88.5 million in RLUSD at 20:41 UTC, which matches the two figures combined.

Ripple has since frozen about $200 million, according to Binance co-CEO Yi He. “Thanks to the Ripple team for promptly freezing approximately 200 million. User Funds are SAFU,” a reference to the exchange’s emergency reserve fund, Yi He wrote on X.

“Ripple is closely monitoring reports of a potential exploit involving Binance and its attackers’ RLUSD holdings and we are actively freezing affected funds,” Jack McDonald, Ripple’s senior vice president for stablecoins, said in a statement. “We’re coordinating with relevant parties and stand ready to support any investigation and appropriate action to keep users protected.”

— Jack McDonald (@_JackMcDonald_) October 29, 2026

Tornado Cash, a mixer that scrambles the trail of crypto payments, appears so far only as funding for the address that set up the attack.

#5209. Hyperliquid’s $224M Paradigm unstaking meets buyback hopes – Can HYPE hold $80?

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Hyperliquid [HYPE] spiked briefly from $78 to $97 on October 29, after Intercontinental Exchange (ICE) announced it would partner with Hyperliquid in introducing a regulated perpetual futures market in the US.

While the processing of this deal has stayed unclear, HYPE has cooled off from the news-driven pump. But major transaction activities are still ongoing. Some institutions are planning to sell while others are buying.

For instance, Paradigm unstaked 2.55 million HYPE tokens valued at $224 million from Hypercore across 15 wallets over the past two days. This amount is equivalent to 17.85% of their staked position, which is at 14.26 million HYPE, worth $1.26 billion.

However, the unstaking from Hypercore does not guarantee automatic selling. But it does matter where the 2.55 million tokens go. If they remain in the wallet, immediate impact is limited, but if moved to exchanges, it could hint at selling.

On the other hand, the chain’s strategic buybacks could mitigate this potential selling pressure.

First, Hyperliquid is expected to receive more than $15 million in November from Circle’s AQAv2 reserve yield framework. In October, almost a similar amount was deposited in USDC and used for buybacks.

Already, the test transfer of 2.52 USDC has reached the Hyperliquid Assistance Fund. Moreover, the protocol collected over $87 million in the month of October, which could add to the buyback program.

However, the revenue and USDC yield account for about half of Paradigm’s unstaked position, though it has not been sold.

Still, it is worth noting that the Bridge Volume has been declining in 2026 compared to the start of Q4 in 2025. Which will carry the day: selling pressure or buying pressure?

On the charts, HYPE is retracing after the news-driven pump. But looking at the market structure on the 4-hour chart, HYPE is bullish as it broke and stayed above $80.

The altcoin remains fairly above this level, but the price could snap back. The CVD shows mild selling with over 450K HYPE tokens sold on Binance’s perpetual futures market.

Similarly, the Open Interest (OI) was declining as the price fell, indicating bears were not aggressively adding positions but were avoiding trading the market.

Therefore, HYPE could revisit $80, which is the last lower high (LH), and coincides with the 0.786 Fibonacci Retracement level. But if the support at $75 holds, the ultimate target for bulls would be $98.

#5208. Tether freezes $4.5 million as Mercado Bitcoin hacker holds $46 million in crypto

Security researchers identified $9.17 million in frozen assets as the Brazilian exchange became the latest target in a wave of attacks following Binance's record $6.3 billion hack.

Tether has frozen more than $4.5 million in USDT linked to a security breach at Brazilian crypto exchange Mercado Bitcoin.

Tether CEO Paolo Ardoino confirmed the freeze on Nov. 2, saying the stablecoin issuer blocked more than 4.5 million USDT linked to the incident.

Notably, Tether is a major investor in the exchange. The stablecoin issuer announced a $20 million strategic investment in Mercado Bitcoin in July to support its expansion into tokenization, payments, and other digital financial services.

Meanwhile, on-chain analyst Specter reported that coordinated efforts involving security researchers and stablecoin issuers had expanded the immobilized assets to more than $9 million across nine digital assets, including USDC, PAXG, RLUSD, and others.

The intervention came as blockchain analytics firm Arkham Intelligence identified approximately $46 million in cryptocurrency held by wallets attributed to the attacker, including $8.37 million in Ethereum, $8.24 million in Bitcoin and $1.76 million in PEPE.

Earlier today, Mercado Bitcoin disclosed that it had identified and contained a security incident affecting one of its systems overnight.

The exchange temporarily suspended deposits and withdrawals while investigating suspicious asset movements but maintained that customers suffered no financial losses and that no personal information was compromised.

However, it has not disclosed the financial extent of the attack or explained whether its corporate reserves were affected.

Mercado Bitcoin subsequently published suspicious destination addresses across Bitcoin, Ethereum-compatible networks, Solana, Stellar and Tron to support tracing and potential recovery efforts.

The Mercado Bitcoin breach comes less than four days after hackers stole a record $6.3 billion from Binance on Oct. 29, extending a series of major attacks against centralized crypto exchanges.

Binance's incident followed a $388 million security breach at Bitget on Sept. 24, when attackers siphoned assets from the exchange's wallet infrastructure. Bitget subsequently tapped its protection fund to absorb the losses and protect customer balances.

The attacks have revived concerns about the security of centralized trading platforms, which collectively custody billions of dollars in digital assets and depend on internal systems to authorize transfers.

A similar pattern emerged in 2025, when two major exchanges suffered substantial losses within weeks of each other.

In February, Bybit lost approximately $1.5 billion after hackers manipulated the transaction approval process for one of its Ethereum cold wallets. The incident followed a $69 million hot-wallet breach at Phemex in January.

Although the incidents involved different attack methods, they exposed vulnerabilities in wallet management, transaction authorization, and the infrastructure exchanges rely on to safeguard customer assets.

The North Korean-linked TraderTraitor operation has been implicated in the two largest attacks. The FBI attributed both the Bybit and Binance thefts to North Korean hackers, demonstrating how sophisticated threat actors have repeatedly targeted centralized custodians.

However, investigators have not established any connection between those operations and the latest Mercado Bitcoin breach.

#5207. NEAR Intents Head Names 3 Survival Rules for Crypto Wallets

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

The end of October 2026 was marked by the largest incident in the history of the crypto market — the $6.3 billion hack of the Binance exchange. This case exposed the vulnerability of the instant exchange (swaps) infrastructure.

While the automated systems of the North Korean group TraderTraitor withdrew $242 million via the Tron blockchain in the first two days, decentralized bridges and liquidity aggregators de facto became the main tool for legalizing the stolen funds.

Against this backdrop, the head of NEAR Intents Alex Shevchenko issued a warning to cryptocurrency wallet developers. His position is pragmatic: focusing exclusively on finding the best rate for the user turns services into unwitting accomplices in cybercrimes.

Since around $3.5 billion still remains under the hackers’ control after a wave of emergency freezes, regulatory pressure on the Web3 sector will inevitably increase. To minimize risks, the head of NEAR Intents formulated three basic survival rules for modern crypto wallets when choosing integration partners:

For wallet developers, the problem is structural. A single swap often passes through several protocols at once — an aggregator, a bridge and multiple liquidity pools — and each hop dilutes responsibility.

If any link in this chain processes tainted funds, the wallet through which the transaction was initiated shares the reputational damage and, increasingly, the legal exposure.

This is why due diligence on integration partners is shifting from an optional step to a core product requirement.

Investments in compliance and integration security are no longer a boring formality. The market has reached a point where the reputational and legal cleanliness of a service is valued far higher than saving a few cents on fees.

XRP’s price readies a hard move as Ali Martinez identifies a long-unseen Bollinger Band Squeeze.

XRP’s price is currently trading in a no-trade zone, according to veteran market analyst Ali Martinez. Currently, the 4-hour XRP price charts present a setup where the Bollinger Bands (BOLL) have squeezed to their narrowest levels since August, 2026.

Last time a similar compression occurred, XRP’s price soared by 71% in 72 hours, but a Bollinger Band squeeze doesn’t necessarily predict price direction, Ali says. A 4-hour candlestick close above $1.27 would restore bullish dominance, while below $1.23 constitutes a bearish trend continuing.

#5205. Binance Finds 'Additional Points of Breach' in $6.3B Hack

Binance Finds 'Additional Points of Breach' in $6.3B Hack

Binance has uncovered "additional points of breach" in Thursday's security incident, which it estimates cost around $6.3 billion, the exchange said on Friday, after initially pointing to a compromised engineer's laptop.

In an announcement late Thursday, Binance said a compromised DevOps engineer's laptop may have allowed an attacker to gain access to a service container and submit unauthorized withdrawal transactions. "We have found no evidence of any wallet private key leak," the exchange said.

Investigation update: Our initial investigation suggested that the security incident may have involved a compromised laptop, however our subsequent investigation has uncovered additional points of breach.

This was a complex attack, and we are doing all we can in coordination… pic.twitter.com/FQQ3vKT6GU

Binance CEO Richard Teng said on Friday that the attacker "did not simply use one engineer's permissions, and this was not a single 'one-click' action," describing "a more complex attack process." "Our investigation has not attributed responsibility to any individual," he wrote.

"This was a complex attack," Binance said in its latest update, adding that it is still gathering the facts. Outflows stopped when it paused withdrawals, and it remains on track to resume them in 45 hours, the exchange said.

I want to speak to you directly about what happened.

Bottom line - your money is safe, and we're covering every loss.

Our initial investigation suggests this security incident may have started with a compromised DevOps engineer's laptop. That gave an attacker access to a…

— Richard Teng (@_RichardTeng) October 30, 2026

About $3 billion of the affected assets has been frozen onchain with the help of blockchain partners, Binance said. The exchange said it will cover all losses from its SAFU fund, an emergency reserve for users, and from company funds, and that "user funds are safe."

Binance initially suspended deposits and withdrawals, revoked the affected address authorization, and is working with law enforcement and blockchain intelligence firms to trace and recover the funds. By Friday morning, deposits were "operating as normal," Teng said, while withdrawals remain suspended as the exchange completes "a security revamp of the withdrawal service and its related addresses."

"We'll share a detailed technical post-mortem once the investigation is complete and every fact is verified—and publish an updated Proof of Reserves once remediation is complete," Teng wrote.

Blockchain analytics firm Arkham put the haul at $6.01 billion, taken from Binance hot wallets on nine blockchains between 4:10 p.m. and 7:05 p.m. ET on Thursday, it said in a tweet. Issuers had frozen $2.80 billion of it, but $2.9 billion in Ethereum, Bitcoin, Tron, Solana and other tokens could still be moved, according to Arkham.

The attacker has kept moving funds. Early Friday, the wallets sent $38.82 million in Ethereum to a new address, while still holding $1.12 billion in ETH, Arkham said. Hours later, a Tron address tagged by Arkham as belonging to the attacker sent about $2.1 million in TRX to nine new addresses in 14 transfers, according to Arkham data.

Stablecoin issuers moved quickly as the funds left Binance. Tether and Circle froze about 975.1 million USDT and 823 million USDC held in the main receiving wallet, worth about $1.8 billion, Decrypt reported on Thursday, when an onchain tally put the drain at $5.57 billion. Binance co-founder Changpeng "CZ" Zhao thanked the two issuers for their "quick freeze."

As of Friday morning, Arkham's entity page for the attacker showed holdings of about 18,270 BTC, 488,364 ETH, 663.6 million TRX and 970,551 SOL, alongside frozen stablecoin balances.

#5204. RUNE sees major sell-offs after FBI’s demand to block Binance hacker transactions sparks debate

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

The Federal Bureau of Investigation is urging private entities, including the node operators of THORChain [RUNE], RPC node operators, instant exchanges, DeFi firms, and blockchain analytics firms, to “block transactions with or derived from addresses (of) TraderTraitor actors.”

The RUNE token has shed 17.7% of its value in the last 24 hours. Over the period, it has also seen a hike in short-selling, as evidenced by an uptick in Open Interest and a drop in funding rates.

On 31st October, THORChain announced that it was halting trading on several blockchains. An Ethereum one-block reorg caused THORChain to pay out 10 ETH that a vault never received.

A reorg happens when the network replaces a recent block with a competing block. Node operators halted all chains as a precaution so developers could investigate. This inadvertently delayed the North Korean hackers.

The FBI has explicitly asked THORChain to block addresses tied to the hack. The chain’s co-founder, Chad Barraford, wrote that “THORNode operators have zero information that isn’t already public and readily available in real time. No subpoenas required.”

Global Head of Business Development and Partnerships at Solv Protocol, Catherine Chan, disagreed with Barraford though.

THORChain co-founder’s argument: the BTC was provided by arbitrageurs, not THORChain. Arbitrageurs sourced it from CEXs, who did the KYC, so it’s the CEXs’ problem. But every BTC sent is signed from THORChain’s TSS vaults. Nodes hold the keys, earn fees, and can vote to halt the chain, i.e. it works like an exchange. Then revenue: 85% ($1.2B) of the Bybit hack was washed into BTC via THORChain. Nodes, LPs and front-ends collected $12M in fees from the Bybit hack. THORChain has to pick whether it is a protocol or an exchange.

As observed previously, the Open Interest ticked higher while the funding rates fell alongside RUNE’s price. This hinted at bearish speculative activity.

The altcoin’s price also slipped below the $0.60-swing low set in late September that kept a bullish trend on the daily timeframe alive. The swift breach of this level signaled a shift in the trend, although the former supply zone at $0.50 has acted as demand in recent hours of trading.

A drop into the $0.41-$0.50 long-term demand zone can be expected next.

#5203. Binance warns of second wave of crypto theft as $6.3 billion hack recovery continues

The exchange has completed reserve verification for 10 cryptocurrencies but says scammers could exploit customers seeking withdrawals and compensation.

Binance has warned customers about potential scams exploiting withdrawal delays as the exchange recovers from a $6.3 billion security breach.

In a Nov. 2 security advisory, the exchange said fraudsters could take advantage of its phased withdrawal reopening by impersonating Binance representatives and offering customers assistance with delayed transactions, compensation claims and asset recovery.

The warning covers fraudulent emails, direct messages, group chats and even small cryptocurrency transfers carrying malicious instructions.

According to Binance, scammers may promise to speed up pending withdrawals, sell customers priority access to withdrawal queues, or instruct them to transfer their holdings into supposedly secure wallets.

The exchange emphasized that it does not charge fees to unlock withdrawals, offer paid recovery services or ask customers to authorize wallet transactions to regain access to their funds.

It also warned against fraudulent proof-of-reserves websites and compensation eligibility links, which could become particularly deceptive as the exchange introduces new verification tools following the breach.

Once customers approve malicious wallet permissions, attackers can transfer their assets without further authorization, potentially creating losses that cannot be reversed.

The warning comes as Binance attempts to restore normal operations after an Oct. 29 attack that compromised its hot wallets and resulted in approximately $6.3 billion in stolen cryptocurrencies.

Meanwhile, Binance announced on Nov. 2 that it had completed the zero-knowledge proof for its Oct. 31 reserve snapshot, allowing customers to independently verify the backing of ten major cryptocurrencies.

The covered assets include Bitcoin, Ethereum, BNB, XRP, Solana, Zcash, and four stablecoins: USDT, USDC, USD1, and RLUSD.

Start with the stories that matter. Get the context behind the headlines and a clear view of what to watch next.

Thanks for signing up to The Catalyst. Look out for your first daily email. If you already receive it, you’re all set.

Binance said the completed proof lets customers verify that their balances were included in the snapshot and backed by reserves of at least 1:1, without revealing individual account holdings.

The process requires users to obtain account-specific verification information and download a verification tool through Binance's official proof-of-reserves platform.

However, the verification applies to balances recorded at 00:00 UTC on Oct. 31 and does not establish the exchange's current financial position or independently audit its overall solvency.

The announcement also comes as Binance continues replenishing assets affected by the theft.

According to the exchange, approximately 95% of the impacted assets have been replaced using company resources, while the remaining 5% consists of less-liquid cryptocurrencies that are more difficult to acquire in sufficient quantities.

Binance has been sourcing those assets through market purchases and over-the-counter transactions, separately from efforts to trace and recover the original stolen funds.

The exchange began restoring withdrawals on Nov. 1, with access returning progressively across supported cryptocurrencies and blockchain networks.

Another phase is scheduled for Nov. 3, although Binance has yet to confirm which additional assets and networks will become available.

The prolonged reopening gives fraudsters an opportunity to exploit customers awaiting access to their funds, particularly through fake support channels and requests for wallet authorizations.

Binance has instructed customers with pending withdrawals to check their status directly through its application and verify suspicious communications using its official verification service.

The exchange plans to publish another reserve snapshot on Nov. 4 covering additional cryptocurrencies. Generating the accompanying zero-knowledge proof is expected to take another 40 to 50 hours.

#5202. Lisk Officially Shuts Down Blockchain After 10 Years

Long-standing blockchain project Lisk has officially ended its blockchain operations after 10 years of existence, moving on to a new financial platform for businesses.

Lisk announced on Nov. 2 the official shutdown of its blockchain after 10 years of existence. This marks the end of one of the longest-lived blockchains in the crypto world.

"Today, the Lisk Chain has officially shut down," the project said in an X post.

With the blockchain shutdown, Lisk has ended its Portal and Bridge services. The rest of the blockchain's infrastructure is also being powered down.

The blockchain shutdown marks a significant milestone in the evolution of the Lisk project, which was launched in 2016 and has seen several changes over the years.

The Lisk team, however, has noted that the shutdown of the blockchain does not spell the end of the Lisk project.

The company is transforming into a new financial platform, one that enables businesses to manage both traditional currencies and stablecoins in one place.

"For 10 years, Lisk built onchain with developers, stakers and holders that made Lisk what it is," Lisk said.

The Lisk project has made it clear that its native token, LSK, will continue to exist even as the blockchain closes shop.

According to the team, LSK will still exist on Ethereum as the loyalty token for its new business financial platform.

In addition, the project announced plans to burn approximately 5.23 million LSK tokens left in its staking rewards pool.

The total figure includes unallocated rewards to users as well as unclaimed earned rewards.

The LSK tokens are set to be burned, permanently removing them from circulation. This will also lower the overall supply of the cryptocurrency.

This comes after a large burn of LSK tokens in September. At the time, Lisk burned 100 million LSK tokens after making the decision to wind down its decentralized autonomous organization (DAO) through a governance vote.

The burn event reduced the LSK token's supply from 400 million to 300 million LSK.

Lisk intends to introduce new use cases for LSK in 2027. This will allow businesses to earn rewards in the token and use LSK for platform fees.

#5201. Trezor Warns CryptoBilis Buyers After Report of Hardware Implant in Safe 5

Trezor Warns CryptoBilis Buyers After Report of Hardware Implant in Safe 5

Hardware wallet manufacturer Trezor has warned customers who bought its devices from reseller CryptoBilis to move their funds, after a buyer reported finding a hardware implant inside a Trezor Safe 5, the company said on Friday.

A customer in Indonesia reported the implant through Trezor's responsible disclosure program on October 28, according to a company blog post. The Safe 5 was bought from CryptoBilis through Indonesian e-commerce platform Tokopedia in August 2026.

We've been made aware, through responsible disclosure, of a single hardware implant in a Trezor Safe 5 device bought from the reseller CryptoBilis in August 2026.

No CryptoBilis buyer has reported a loss of funds to us.

IMPORTANT: If you bought a Trezor from… pic.twitter.com/TiaKfHBnX2

The report describes an extra circuit board fitted between the device's main board and its screen, with a cellular module and an antenna. According to the report, which Trezor said it has not verified, the board reads what the screen shows, including the wallet backup, and sends it out over a mobile network whenever the device is plugged in.

Trezor has not yet received the device, but said it is treating the report as credible based on the customer's description and a video interview. "What we've seen looks sophisticated enough that we decided to warn customers now," it said. "Waiting for lab confirmation would leave CryptoBilis buyers exposed in the meantime."

No CryptoBilis buyer has reported a loss of funds to Trezor, the company said.

Trezor urged anyone who bought one of its wallets from CryptoBilis from March 2026 onwards to move their funds to a newly created wallet with a new recovery phrase as soon as possible, and not to reuse the old phrase on any device. Buyers who have not yet set up their device should not use it. Affected customers can keep their CryptoBilis device and contact Trezor Support for a replacement.

Trezor removed CryptoBilis as a reseller on October 9, after rival hardware wallet manufacturer Ledger began investigating losses among customers who bought its devices from the Southeast Asian reseller. Trezor has since inspected four of its devices bought from CryptoBilis, a Safe 7, two Safe 5s and a Safe 3, and found no evidence of tampering.

Ledger's Donjon security team found an implant in Ledger devices sold through CryptoBilis that reads the 24-word recovery phrase from the screen during setup and sends it out over LTE. Trezor's blog says the newly reported implant "appears to operate similarly," though the company added that it won't draw comparisons while investigations are ongoing and doesn't know who is behind it.

Ledger has said that all confirmed losses involve devices bought from CryptoBilis, and asked resellers in seven Asian markets to pull its devices. One of the largest victims was a scammer who lost more than 60 BTC, worth about $4.8 million, according to blockchain analytics firm Chainalysis.

Trezor has also had to warn customers about partner failures before. In August, it said a breach at shipping provider ShipMonk had exposed the names, phone numbers and home addresses of nearly 14,000 U.S. customers, a figure that grew by another 67,000 in September.

#5200. Evernorth’s Warrants Could Raise $135 Million.. Or Not

Home » Trendsetter News » Evernorth’s Warrants Could Raise $135 Million.. Or Not

With millions of shares becoming tradable, financing terms & decisions will shape the company’s ability to expand holdings.

Evernorth could receive roughly $135 million from outstanding warrants, or nothing, depending on how they are exercised. In a new crypto research, a popular analyst examining the XRP treasury company’s financing argues that this distinction matters more than its stock’s reported one-day collapse of roughly 50%.

The company holds about 473 million XRP, according to Fire Hustle. Its ability to expand that position depends partly on its share price, financing terms and whether investors decide to sell newly tradable stock.

Fire Hustle identifies approximately 11.7 million warrants, each carrying an $11.50 exercise price. Full exercise for cash would bring Evernorth roughly $135 million, potentially funding additional XRP purchases.

A sustained share price of at least $18 – on 20 trading days within a 30-trading-day period – would allow the company to call the warrants, subject to the terms described in the video. Holders would then face a deadline to exercise before the warrants were redeemed for a nominal amount.

But management could require cashless exercise instead. That would produce fewer new shares while bringing in no exercise proceeds. “The option that Evernorth picks,” Fire Hustle says, determines whether this financing route delivers fresh purchasing power for XRP.

The distinction is important: a rising stock price alone would not guarantee another large XRP purchase.

According to the YouTube video, cash investors including SBI Holdings could sell more than 13 million shares once the relevant resale registration becomes effective.

Fire Hustle says those investors did not sign the six-month lockups covering Ripple, a Larsen family trust and Arrington’s fund.

That potential supply is substantial beside the fewer than five million shares the analyst says remained publicly held after heavy redemptions by the blank-check company’s investors.

Still, eligibility to sell is not evidence of an imminent exit. The analyst estimates SBI paid in the mid-to-high teens per share and notes its longstanding relationship with Ripple, suggesting less incentive to sell near its entry price.

A premium to those assets can support further purchases; a discount can undermine that strategy and make buybacks more attractive.

Fire Hustle raises XRP sales as a possible consequence, not an announced company plan. Investors therefore have two distinct developments to watch: when restricted shares become tradable, and whether warrant exercises actually put cash into Evernorth.

#5199. Tom Lee calls Ethereum’s 4% post-hack fall bullish

The BitMine chairman calls ETH's limited decline constructive, but his 20% Bybit comparison covers a longer period. BitMine has kept buying Ethereum.

Tom Lee believes crypto's muted reaction to Binance's $6.3 billion hack is another sign that a bull market is underway.

The BitMine chairman pointed to Ethereum's roughly 4% decline following the Oct. 29 theft, which was more than four times larger than the $1.5 billion stolen from Bybit in February 2025.

Ethereum fell 8% on the day the Bybit breach was disclosed and extended its losses to 20% within a week, according to Lee. The 20% comparison covers a full week after Bybit, while the Binance figure covers only the days since Oct. 29.

In a Nov. 2 statement, Lee described the latest market reaction as “very constructive,” arguing that the industry's response to an unprecedented security breach reflects growing resilience.

The prices quoted in BitMine's releases show Ethereum fell approximately 17% between Oct. 4 and Nov. 1.

Lee cited six factors behind the relatively subdued market response, including Binance's ability to cover customer losses, swift intervention by stablecoin issuers and the absence of cascading failures across decentralized finance platforms.

The exchange initially committed more than $6 billion in company funds to cover the stolen assets, seeking to reassure customers that the theft would not leave them exposed to losses.

By Nov. 2, Binance said it had replenished approximately 95% of the affected assets using its own funding sources, including its corporate treasury and insurance reserves.

Binance also said users could independently verify that ten major cryptocurrencies, including Bitcoin, Ethereum, XRP and BNB, were backed at least 1:1 in its Oct. 31 proof-of-reserves snapshot.

Blockchain analytics firm Elliptic verified approximately $2.85 billion in frozen or seized assets, accounting for 44% of the theft.

Tether and Circle were among the companies that restricted compromised tokens, while other issuers and blockchain networks took similar measures.

Lee also highlighted the speed of the criminal investigation. The FBI identified North Korea's TraderTraitor hacking group as responsible within two days, compared with previous incidents in which attribution took considerably longer.

Another distinction was the nature of the breach. Investigators have linked the Binance incident to compromised corporate infrastructure, rather than a failure in Ethereum or another blockchain's underlying protocol.

BitMine has continued buying Ethereum despite its recent downturn.

The company acquired another 8,573 ETH during the latest reporting week, lifting its holdings to 6,060,506 tokens, equivalent to approximately 4.9% of Ethereum's circulating supply.

However, the value of its broader portfolio of crypto assets, cash, marketable securities and other investments declined from $17.4 billion on Oct. 4 to $14.5 billion on Nov. 1, despite the continued accumulation.

Elliptic estimated on Nov. 2 that approximately $3 billion remained in addresses linked to the theft, mostly as Bitcoin and Ethereum.

Binance is also working to source the remaining 5% of affected customer assets, comprising less-liquid tokens, while preparing another proof-of-reserves snapshot for Nov. 4.

#5198. Near Protocol (NEAR), XRP, Zcash (ZEC) and Shiba Inu (SHIB) Price Analysis for November 3: Is Recovery Still Possible?

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

Near Protocol (NEAR): NEAR is correcting below $4.50, and $4.00 is the key support.

XRP (XRP): XRP stays bearish near $1.25 while it trades below $1.35.

Zcash (ZEC): ZEC is consolidating between $1,150 and $1,350 above its major moving averages.

Shiba Inu (SHIB): SHIB remains bearish near $0.00000464, below every major moving average.

NEAR Protocol is in a short-term bearish correction after its September rally, which took the price from about $1.80 to nearly $5.90 in October. Since that local peak, NEAR has been consolidating, with lower highs and growing selling pressure.

The latest daily candles show bullish momentum fading. NEAR trades around $4.10, below its short-term moving averages. Losing the 4.40–4.50 region matters because it had been acting as dynamic support, and its breakdown suggests sellers are gaining control of the immediate trend.

The wider structure is still constructive, though. NEAR holds above its longer-term moving averages, including the 100-day and 200-day, so the larger bullish trend hasn't been invalidated yet.

The RSI has fallen toward the 40–45 region. Momentum is deteriorating but not oversold, which leaves room for more downside before a meaningful technical recovery.

Key support sits at $4.00, then $3.80 and $3.60. A decisive daily close below $4.00 could speed up selling toward the rising 100-day moving average.

On the upside, NEAR needs to reclaim 4.40–4.50 to stabilize its near-term structure. A sustained breakout above $4.70 would strengthen the recovery case and could open the way to 5.00–5.30.

Outlook: NEAR stays vulnerable to more downside while it trades below $4.50. Holding the 3.80–4.00 support zone could, though, lay the groundwork for renewed accumulation. The broader uptrend is intact, but buyers need to show stronger momentum before a sustainable bullish continuation becomes likely.

XRP's technical structure keeps weakening after its October recovery attempt, which briefly pushed the price toward $1.60. The rejection that followed produced a run of lower highs and lower lows, so bearish momentum has retaken control.

XRP now trades near $1.25, below every major moving average on the daily chart. Short-term and longer-term trend indicators all sit overhead as resistance.

The 1.30–1.35 region matters because it used to be a consolidation area. XRP couldn't hold it, which suggests buyers lack the momentum to reverse the downtrend.

After a sharp decline, the price has stabilized near 1.24–1.25. Volume on the latest candles is fairly modest, though, so there's little sign of aggressive accumulation.

The RSI sits in the lower part of its neutral range, around 35–40, which points to sustained selling pressure. It is approaching oversold territory but hasn't produced a convincing bullish reversal signal.

Immediate support is $1.20, with further downside targets around $1.15 and $1.10. A break below $1.20 could bring another wave of selling.

On the upside, reclaiming $1.30 would be a first improvement, but XRP would need a move above 1.35–1.40 to challenge the bearish structure. The $1.40 area stands out because several moving averages converge nearby.

Outlook: XRP stays bearish in the short term as long as it trades below $1.35. Stabilizing around 1.20–1.25 could prompt a technical rebound, but a real trend reversal needs stronger buying volume and a decisive recovery above key resistance. Until then, rallies remain vulnerable to renewed selling.

Zcash is consolidating after its September rally, which took the price from about $500 to nearly $1,700 in early October. Heavy selling near the peak sent ZEC sharply lower before it settled into a range between $1,150 and $1,350.

On the latest daily candles, buyers are trying to hold the price around $1,250. They have repeatedly failed to sustain momentum above $1,300, so short-term bullish conviction is limited. Neither side has decisive control right now.

Technically, ZEC is above its longer-term moving averages, which keeps the broader bullish structure in place. The rising 100-day and 200-day averages support the underlying uptrend despite the correction.

Shorter-term moving averages are converging around the current range. That suggests volatility compression, which could come before a significant directional move.

The RSI hovers near the neutral 50 level, so momentum is balanced. Volume is down from September's breakout, which points to less speculative participation during the consolidation.

Key support is $1,200, then $1,150 and $1,050. A break below $1,150 would weaken the bullish structure and could speed up losses toward the 100-day moving average.

Immediate resistance is 1,300–1,350. A confirmed breakout above that zone could bring renewed buying, with targets at $1,400 and eventually $1,500.

Outlook: ZEC keeps a cautiously constructive outlook while it holds above 1,150–1,200. Near-term momentum is subdued, but consolidation above the major moving averages suggests the broader uptrend is technically intact. A sustained recovery above $1,350 would give stronger confirmation of bullish continuation.

Shiba Inu remains under sustained bearish pressure after failing to hold its September recovery. A rally toward $0.00000620 gave way to a prolonged correction of progressively lower highs and weakening buying momentum.

The latest daily chart shows SHIB breaking below an ascending support trendline that dates from August. Losing it removes an important structural support and reinforces the bearish outlook.

At around $0.00000464, SHIB trades below all major moving averages. The 20-day, 50-day, 100-day and 200-day averages all sit above the price, stacking up layers of overhead resistance.

The recent decline toward $0.00000450 was followed by a modest attempt to stabilize, but low buying volume suggests demand is still too weak for a convincing reversal.

The RSI is around 35–40, showing persistent bearish momentum and nearing oversold conditions. That raises the chance of a short-term relief rally, but it doesn't by itself confirm a sustainable recovery.

Immediate support is $0.00000450, then $0.00000430 and $0.00000410. A sustained break below $0.00000450 could bring more selling and a possible retest of its summer lows.

On the upside, initial resistance is 0.00000490–0.00000500. A recovery above $0.00000520 would improve short-term structure, while $0.00000550 remains a significant longer-term resistance level.

Outlook: SHIB keeps a bearish technical outlook while it trades below $0.00000520. The ascending-support breakdown and bearish moving-average positioning suggest downside risks remain elevated. A meaningful recovery would need stronger volume and a sustained reclaim of previously lost support levels.

#5197. TRM and Chainalysis Tie $6.4B Binance Hack to North Korea's TraderTraitor

TRM and Chainalysis Tie $6.4B Binance Hack to North Korea's TraderTraitor

Three blockchain analytics firms have attributed this week's hack of Binance to North Korea, with two of the largest naming TraderTraitor, a North Korean hacking group, as responsible for what both describe as the largest crypto theft on record.

TRM Labs said it made the attribution "with high confidence," based on "robust on and off-chain intelligence." It put the theft at about $6.36 billion, moved out of Binance wallets on 10 blockchains, most of it in about 20 minutes from 4:05 p.m. ET on Thursday. That is about four times the $1.5 billion Bybit hack of February 2025, which the FBI attributed to the same group, TRM noted.

Chainalysis, which put the total at about $6.4 billion, said the strongest evidence came when its investigators saw funds stolen from Binance commingling in real time with funds from last month's Bitget exploit, which had been attributed to North Korean actors.

A Chainalysis spokesperson told CNN the firm had "high confidence" North Korea was behind the hack. Singapore-based Pangolin Intelligence also told CNN it believed a North Korean hacking unit was responsible, based on "analyzing the on-chain activity related to this hack and correlating with information gathered from previous known incidents." North Korea has previously denied hacking allegations.

TRM and Chainalysis both said the attacker's operational wallet was funded through Tornado Cash, a crypto mixer. TRM said that and other features, including staging that began well before the theft, matched earlier North Korea-linked attacks such as the Bybit hack and April's KelpDAO exploit.

On Ethereum, the attacker used an EIP-7702 delegation, a feature introduced in Ethereum's Pectra upgrade that lets an ordinary wallet run smart contract code, to turn a Binance hot wallet into a sweeper it controlled, with 50 pre-signed authorizations prepared in advance, according to TRM. Binance wallets on the other nine chains sent funds to the attacker through ordinary signed transfers. In TRM's assessment, that points to a compromise of Binance's hot wallet signing infrastructure.

Binance has said its investigation began with a compromised engineer's laptop, but has since uncovered "additional points of breach." The Bybit attack also began with a single compromised machine: North Korean hackers planted malicious code in the infrastructure of wallet provider Safe after compromising a Safe developer's computer, Decrypt reported at the time.

The attacker has moved quickly to launder what it could not keep. Chainalysis said it is seeing "never-seen-before laundering patterns," with thousands of transactions in a short period suggesting the use of automation to circulate funds repeatedly among addresses. The attacker also sold USDT to market makers and borrowed ETH against USDT on lending protocol Aave to race issuers' freezes, according to TRM, and now holds more than 420,000 ETH and 18,270 BTC, about $2.4 billion beyond any issuer's reach.

Tether, Circle, Ripple and World Liberty Financial have frozen about $2.67 billion in stablecoins, and Arbitrum's Security Council froze 8,426 ETH and 74.6 million ARB, TRM said. Binance puts the total frozen at about $3 billion.

Binance has begun recovering affected assets for users and continues to work with law enforcement, issuers, blockchain intelligence firms and industry partners to trace, freeze and recover additional funds.

The company has already publicly committed to cover losses through… pic.twitter.com/UqtBd37Fpj

In an update on Friday, Binance said it has reserved more than $6.34 billion of company funds to cover the incident, $5 billion of which is already held in the exact tokens that were stolen. It is buying the rest through market and over-the-counter channels, which "can take longer because some tokens may have low liquidity," the exchange said. Those figures do not include frozen assets.

"We will weather this storm," Binance said.

North Korean hackers have long targeted the exchange. In August 2025, Binance chief security officer Jimmy Su told Decrypt that North Korean attackers try to get hired at Binance every day, and called them the single biggest threat to crypto companies. On Wednesday, the U.S. Treasury sanctioned 10 individuals and one entity over North Korean IT worker schemes that moved millions of dollars in crypto.

#5196. Coinbase (COIN) Misses Revenue Estimates as Users Fall, Armstrong Sets Up New Share Sales

Coinbase missed revenue expectations as users declined, despite growth in recurring revenue, while executive share-sale plans and a massive Binance security breach added pressure to crypto markets.

Coinbase had a mixed third quarter. Revenue came in at $1.20 billion, below the $1.233 billion analysts expected, according to company filings. Subscription and services revenue did better, reaching $570 million. The company is still heavily tied to trading activity, but its recurring income is growing.

Monthly Transacting Users fell to 7.4 million, down from 9.3 million a year earlier. During its earnings call, Coinbase blamed overall market conditions. Its share of crypto trading volume did rise to 10.7%, the fourth straight quarter of gains, which suggests it is winning business even as the broader market quiets.

JUST IN: Coinbase disclosed buying 643 Bitcoin worth $1.5 billion for its balance sheet in Q3 🚀 pic.twitter.com/5BYdrwLCJN

Separately, filings reported on October 30 show CEO Brian Armstrong has set up a plan to sell up to 8.17 million Class A shares. He adopted the plan in August under SEC Rule 10b5-1, which lets executives schedule sales ahead of time. Sales could run from November 16 through October 2027, subject to stock price conditions. Co-founder Fred Ehrsam and President Emilie Choi have similar plans, bringing the total to about 13.6 million shares.

This is not new territory for Armstrong. In the past, VanEck analyst Matthew Sigel tracked about $545.7 million in sales over nine months, starting in April 2025. Armstrong has said these sales are part of his risk management, not a signal about the company.

Coinbase shares (COIN) fell about 15% today, but not necessarily due to this earnings report.

Binance disclosed a security breach on October 29 that it estimates at $6.3 billion, making it the largest crypto theft on record. Around $3 billion in assets has been frozen so far, with industry partners assisting in the freezes.

The hackers are allegedly linked to North Korea, though Binance has not attributed the attack to any group. The exchange says it has found no evidence of a leaked wallet private key.

Binance says it has $6.34 billion in reserves available to cover losses. Deposits were unaffected, and the company expects to resume withdrawals within 48 hours. Figures remain preliminary, and estimates vary slightly across outlets.

The market reacted quickly. Bitcoin fell roughly 4.5% to below $77,000. Shares of Strategy (MSTR) fell nearly 10% on the day. Strive (ASST) shares fell over 12% at times. Coinbase’s stock is down over 55% on the year.
