#5195. Binance can cut a winning Bitcoin hedge even when customer assets are backed

Binance says only surplus futures reserves were reallocated, while ADL rules can still reduce an opposing profitable position.

Binance's latest reserve proof addresses whether a customer's Bitcoin is backed at a dated snapshot. A futures hedge faces a separate test: Binance's futures rules allow Auto-Deleveraging, or ADL, to reduce a profitable short that was offsetting the holding.

In its Nov. 2 proof-completion update, Binance said holders of ten named tokens, including Bitcoin, could independently verify backing of at least 1:1 for the snapshot taken at 00:00 UTC on Oct. 31.

The exchange had disclosed in its Oct. 31 recovery update that some futures insurance reserves helped replenish assets stolen in its security incident. Binance said it released only excess above its required coverage level and that the remaining funds exceeded that requirement. The disclosure alone establishes neither a reserve shortfall nor a measurable increase in ADL probability.

Binance also distinguished its Futures Insurance Funds from user assets: they are its own reserves, outside the user-verifiable PoR view. Those reserves support the handling of bankrupt futures positions and reduce the need to close opposing traders' positions.

Binance said it monitors those funds against stress scenarios and adds company funds when required.

The futures insurance FAQ describes several pools assigned to different contract categories, with Binance able to change allocations. An exchange-wide reserve figure therefore cannot automatically describe the protection available to every Bitcoin contract.

The same FAQ defines Maximum Takeover Capacity as a cap on the notional, or face, value of bankrupt positions a fund can absorb at a time. It is a multiple of the fund's current balance, with the predetermined multiplier kept confidential.

That capacity limit differs from the amount available to absorb losses. Its FAQ says ADL can be engaged when takeover capacity is reached or when unrealized losses on positions assumed by the fund exceed the relevant available balance. Waiting for a fund balance to reach zero would miss the capacity constraint described in its guidance.

Neither the amount released nor the information needed to calculate current contract-specific headroom is established by the reallocation disclosure.

Under the ADL priority rules, opposing profitable positions can be selected when the fund cannot accept a bankrupt position. Profitability and leverage affect their place in the queue. A position can therefore be winning on price and still be selected for reduction.

Consider a purely hypothetical trader holding 1 BTC and a linear futures short of 1 BTC. Assume the short has become profitable after a price decline. If ADL cuts it to 0.5 BTC while the underlying holding stays unchanged, only half the holding remains offset.

If Bitcoin then falls by an assumed $10,000 per BTC, the remaining combined position loses $5,000 on that further move. That calculation assumes spot and futures move together, quantities remain fixed and the trader adds no replacement hedge. It excludes fees, funding, basis differences, taxes and collateral changes.

Start with the stories that matter. Get the context behind the headlines and a clear view of what to watch next.

Thanks for signing up to The Catalyst. Look out for your first daily email. If you already receive it, you’re all set.

The $5,000 is an incremental loss after the reduction, not the strategy's total profit or loss. Whatever the closed portion earned does not provide an ongoing offset to later price changes in the holding.

For positions governed by Binance RCH's clearing rules, an ADL-selected position can be reduced or closed at the triggering bankrupt position's bankruptcy price, without specific consent from the affected clearing participant.

This differs from the collateral problem in CryptoSlate's October analysis of fragmented hedges, where an underfunded losing leg can be liquidated while profits sit elsewhere. ADL can remove the profitable opposing leg. Either mechanism can separate positions that were intended to work together.

Binance's Delta Neutral Account guidance offers qualifying hedged positions lower ADL priority within the same profit/loss category. It does not exempt those positions from ADL.

Initial qualification requires Portfolio Margin or Portfolio Margin Pro, VIP4 or above and an enabled Delta Mode for the individual account. The gap between the calculated long and short exposures must be less than 5% of the larger side, expressed as a relative difference below 0.05. Enabling a master account does not enable its subaccounts.

The calculation recognizes eligible assets in Cross Margin and Futures accounts, Cross Margin debts and futures positions. Assets in the Spot Account and options positions are excluded. A Bitcoin holding and an offsetting futures short may form an economic hedge without automatically qualifying for this special queue treatment.

There is also a distinction between entry and continuing eligibility: the FAQ says a later fall below VIP4 alone changes nothing, while disabling Portfolio Margin disables Delta Mode.

Contract scope matters alongside account status. Within the Binance RCH rulebook, ADL applies contract by contract: a position in one contract type is not used to cover losses in another. Those rules refer to Default Funds and tie potentially affected positions to the relevant fund's coverage and available capacity.

The FAQs are educational guidance that defer to governing rules and contract specifications. The applicable Binance entity, product terms and account arrangement determine which legal protections govern a position.

#5194. Binance Restores Withdrawals for Ripple's RLUSD Stablecoin

Cryptocurrency exchange Binance has fully enabled withdrawals for Ripple’s RLUSD stablecoin on the Ethereum network. This move is part of the exchange's gradual service recovery process after the massive $6.3 billion hack.

On Nov. 3, the exchange released an update on the next stage of its withdrawal restoration plan, with a new list of cryptocurrencies available for withdrawal.

Interestingly, the new list of assets includes Ripple’s US dollar-backed stablecoin RLUSD and a number of other stablecoins.

This announcement follows Binance’s gradual restoration of withdrawals, which began on Nov. 1 after a brief halt due to one of the largest hacks in the crypto industry.

As per the latest recovery update from Binance, the exchange began its second significant withdrawal restoration phase at 04:00 UTC on Nov. 3.

Apart from RLUSD, the cryptocurrency exchange is now gradually re-enabling withdrawals for dozens of cryptocurrencies on multiple blockchain networks, including Ethereum, BNB Smart Chain, Solana, XRP Ledger, Sui, Arbitrum, Optimism, Tron and Base.

For Ethereum, the list of cryptocurrencies now available for withdrawal includes Shiba Inu (SHIB), Chainlink (LINK), Worldcoin (WLD), Ondo (ONDO) and Ethena (ENA).

At the same time, the cryptocurrency exchange has also added XRP and a number of other top crypto assets to its list of withdrawals that will soon be re-enabled on BNB Smart Chain.

The crypto exchange emphasized that not all of the listed assets will be enabled for withdrawal at once. Instead, each asset and network combination will be re-enabled individually after the necessary security and operational checks are completed.

“We will gradually re-enable more assets & networks in stages once we complete the relevant security checks,” Binance said.

At the same time, the crypto exchange also warned that there may be delays due to traffic during this restoration process. Meanwhile, cryptocurrency deposits remain functional.

Initially, Binance started re-enabling withdrawals on Nov. 1 for top cryptocurrencies, including Bitcoin (BTC), Ethereum (ETH), XRP, Solana (SOL), Dogecoin (DOGE) and Zcash (ZEC), as well as major stablecoins USDT and USDC.

The latest withdrawal restoration update comes only a few days after the cryptocurrency exchange suffered a massive hack on Oct. 29, which resulted in the theft of about $6.3 billion in crypto.

The FBI officially identified the perpetrators of the hack as North Korean hackers linked to the infamous TraderTraitor operation on Oct. 31.

The U.S. agency revealed that hackers have been converting stolen cryptocurrencies mostly into Ethereum as a potential path toward laundering the assets through decentralized finance platforms and crypto swap services.

More withdrawal restorations will follow as Binance moves forward with its security checks and recovery processes.

#5193. Morning Minute: Binance’s $6.4B Hack Overshadows Hyperliquid’s Biggest Day

Morning Minute: Binance’s $6.4B Hack Overshadows Hyperliquid’s Biggest Day

Morning Minute is a daily newsletter written by Tyler Warner. The analysis and opinions expressed are his own and do not necessarily reflect those of Decrypt. And check out our daily news show 'FOMO HOUR' covering all of the top stories and market action.

Someone stole about $6.4 billion from Binance. That is, by a wide margin, the biggest crypto theft that has ever happened, four times the $1.5 billion taken from Bybit in February 2025.

The money left across three different blockchains: roughly $3.5 billion in ETH, $1.4 billion in BTC, and $630 million on Tron (final accounting still underway).

Luckily, stablecoin issuers can freeze their own tokens; it’s a kill switch built into USDT and USDC. Tether and Circle used it, locking up about $2.39 billion between them. That’s 43% of the haul. That leaves somewhere between $2.6 and $3.2 billion still out there.

Aave also stepped in, freezing its V3 and V4 markets on Ethereum. That’s precautionary, given the worry is an attacker using stolen money to borrow against and leave the protocol holding bad debt.

As this was playing out, Binance stated it’s “aware of reports of a potential security issue” and that user funds are “completely safe,” and paused withdrawals on multiple networks. The clearest comment came from CZ, who no longer runs Binance but is its largest shareholder: it “does look like a hack,” and he expects user funds to be “fully covered.” Later in the evening, Binance announced that it has reserved over $6.34B in company funds to cover losses. So funds are indeed “safu.”

ZachXBT shared that the Binance hack funds were commingled with funds from the $388M Bitget exploit, leading him to believe it’s the same hacker group out of North Korea behind the attack. As for how it happened, Binance pointed to a compromised DevOps engineer’s laptop which was compromised, allowing the attacks to submit the unauthorized transactions, before identifying "additional points of breach" in the following hours.

Markets did what you’d expect. Bitcoin fell below $797,000, ETH fell sub-$2,200 and BNB sub-$700. Over $1.33B in positions got liquidated on the day.

Unfortunately, this came after what was shaping up to be one of the most positive days for a crypto project in its history.

Earlier on Thursday, Trade.xyz announced that Intercontinental Exchange (the company that owns the New York Stock Exchange) is investing in it. The plan is a regulated U.S. venue offering round-the-clock perpetual futures built on Hyperliquid: stocks, indices, commodities, currencies and crypto, with its own exchange and clearing setup, pending CFTC approval.

Stanley Druckenmiller is joining as well, the first of a small group of outside market figures. He already owns the trade; his family office disclosed a $23 million position in Hyperliquid Strategies earlier this year.

So Hyperliquid has its path to a fully regulated perps offering inside the U.S., claims Stanley Druckenmiller as an investor (and a tout on X), and ICE’s CEO said that Hyperliquid was “bigger than NASDAQ.” Doesn’t get much better than that.

I am excited to be an investor in trade(xyz) as they partner with ICE to bring 24/7 perpetual futures on Hyperliquid to the United States. As a market participant, I am looking forward to to using their innovative product to enhance my investment process.

— Stan Druckenmiller (@standuquesne) October 29, 2026

HYPE shot as high as $97 on the day, up more than 20% at peak, before falling to $89 in the Binance hack aftermath. As of this morning, it’s at $93 and still up 8% on the day.

Unfortunately, now we have to deal with the Binance hack aftermath. Hyperliquid is a likely winner as a primary DEX option vs CEXs like Binance (which will likely be less trusted now.) But there’s also a good chance of the entire market being dragged down by fears of more exploits looming.

We will find out soon enough. Expect a bumpy ride…

#5192. Kinetic’s $24 million XRP-linked lending market showed a 0.04% base rate before rewards

Kinetic’s November 1 snapshots separate modest FXRP loan demand from collateral use and incentive-supported returns.

Kinetic’s FXRP lending market on Flare showed roughly $24 million supplied on Nov. 1, yet the base annualized rate for lenders was about 0.04% before rewards. For XRP holders, the supplied balance alone is a weak measure of borrower-funded income.

Kinetic is a lending protocol on Flare, where FXRP represents XRP-linked exposure through the FAssets system. Its official market page displayed $24 million of FXRP supplied and $2 million borrowed in the Nov. 1 observation.

The paired stablecoin market told a different story. FlareSignal’s Nov. 1 lending snapshot put FXRP utilization at 6.7%, against 92.8% for USDT0. The gap helps explain why demand to borrow a token, demand to borrow against it, and the income paid to its holders require separate measures.

The FXRP supply APR in that snapshot was about 0.04%, while the FXRP borrower APR was about 0.7%. The supply figure describes a simple annualized base rate at the observation time.

Rewards change the displayed return. In the Nov. 1 capture, Kinetic’s official FXRP row showed a 0.59% supply APY and a separately displayed 0.55% rFLR reward component. The reward component belongs inside the quoted yield comparison; adding it again to the headline supply APY would overstate the display.

DefiLlama’s specific isoFXRP pool page separately labeled its 0.57% supply APY as a 30-day average, alongside a 0.04% base component and a +0.54% reward component labeled WFLR. Rounding and differing observation windows limit direct reconciliation with the app’s figures.

DefiLlama’s Kinetic adapter calculates base yield from supply rates and rewards from separate emissions and token prices. That separation identifies the economic question behind the headline: how much comes from borrowers paying for credit, and how much comes from incentive distributions? The observed reward component was much larger than the quoted base rate.

FlareSignal reported about 19.45 million FXRP supplied and 1.31 million FXRP borrowed in the dated observation. It also reported about 5.04 million USDT0 supplied and 4.68 million USDT0 borrowed. The utilization figures show substantially different demand for borrowing the two assets within the isolated market.

Supplied FXRP may also support borrowing when enabled as collateral and permitted by market rules. Kinetic’s lending documentation describes suppliers enabling assets as collateral to borrow other assets, with borrowers required to remain overcollateralized. Someone seeking stablecoin liquidity may value an XRP-linked collateral position while having little use for an additional FXRP loan.

Collateral utility gives the holder access to borrowing. Any cash return to a supplier remains a separate benefit. A holder may value access to credit while receiving little base interest on the supplied asset.

The USDT0 figure reports aggregate market debt. Kinetic’s isolated-market contract directory identifies the market’s assets and infrastructure, but attributing that debt to FXRP collateral requires individual account data.

XRP is the XRP Ledger’s native asset, while the asset supplied to this Kinetic market is FXRP on Flare. That distinction places additional protocol steps between holding native XRP and having a lending position.

Flare’s XRP minting documentation describes sending underlying XRP to the Core Vault on the XRP Ledger, followed by an executor finalizing the FXRP mint on Flare using verified payment evidence. The FAssets system connects that representation to underlying assets and collateral arrangements.

The return journey has conditions too. Under the ordinary holder redemption process, FAssets are burned and underlying payment is initiated through the protocol. Fees and redemption-ticket limits can apply. If an agent fails to pay, compensation can involve collateral after proof of nonpayment.

Kinetic’s Nov. 1 observations show substantial FXRP supplied relative to FXRP loans, with a reward component much larger than base lender interest. For this lending market, the test for lasting demand is continued use of credit and the interest borrowers pay for it. The supplied balance establishes market size; those cash flows would establish a stronger foundation for lender income.

#5191. Israel Extradites Alleged Nomad Bridge Exploiter to US Over $2.8M Theft: Report

Israel Extradites Alleged Nomad Bridge Exploiter to US Over $2.8M Theft: Report

Israel has extradited an Israeli-Russian citizen to the United States to face charges over the alleged theft of about $2.8 million from crypto bridge Nomad, Israel's State Attorney's Office announced on Thursday, according to a report in The Jerusalem Post.

Alexander Block, formerly known as Alexander Gurevich, was transferred to U.S. authorities early Thursday after unsuccessful appeals in Israel. He was arrested at Ben Gurion Airport in May 2025 while preparing to fly to Russia, shortly after changing his surname and obtaining a passport in his new name.

A federal grand jury in the Northern District of California indicted Block in August 2023 on four counts of computer fraud, one count of transporting stolen property and three counts of money laundering, according to Israel's Supreme Court judgment in the case. The U.S. requested his extradition in December 2024.

According to the U.S. extradition request, as set out in the judgment, a June 2022 software update left a flaw in Nomad's smart contracts that let users receive tokens on one blockchain without locking up the equivalent assets on another. Block, operating under the username "Bitlik," allegedly made six test transfers before carrying out four main transfers around August 1, 2022, receiving tokens worth more than $2.8 million. He then allegedly moved the tokens through several wallets and converted them into other cryptocurrencies to conceal their origin.

His actions exposed the flaw to other Nomad users, who drained a Nomad-controlled wallet holding more than $186 million in user funds, the request alleges. A few days later, Block returned some of the tokens and contacted a senior Nomad official on Telegram, offering to return the rest in exchange for payment for finding the flaw. He later cut off contact, and Nomad went to U.S. law enforcement.

The Jerusalem District Court approved Block's extradition in July 2025. On appeal, he argued that the transactions used functions available within Nomad's automated system, and that the public record of blockchain transfers undermined the money laundering allegations.

The Supreme Court rejected both arguments, holding that exploiting a programming flaw could constitute unlawful computer intrusion even where the software technically permitted the action, The Jerusalem Post reported. It also found that publicly visible transactions do not necessarily identify the people behind wallets or rule out efforts to conceal where funds came from.

Supreme Court President Isaac Amit later rejected Block's request for a further hearing, according to the State Attorney's Office.

The August 2022 exploit drained Nomad of nearly all its funds. Because it took little technical knowledge to copy the original attack, Paradigm researcher Sam Sun likened what followed to "a frenzied free-for-all," Decrypt reported at the time.

In December 2025, the Federal Trade Commission proposed a settlement with Illusory Systems, Nomad's operator, saying the bridge lost $186 million after hackers exploited a poorly tested software update. The settlement would require the company to return recovered funds to users who have not been reimbursed. Nomad recovered about $22 million in the days after the hack.

#5189. Ethena’s trading team can approve borrowers of assets backing USDe under new rules

The October 29 framework delegates qualifying borrower screening while retaining committee call-in powers and oversight of lending limits.

Ethena’s October 29 institutional-lending framework sets out a route for its trading team to onboard qualifying borrowers of assets backing USDe without an individual Risk Committee vote.

The posted rules describe a change in approval authority over lending that helps back Ethena’s synthetic dollar. For USDe holders, published eligibility rules and exposure disclosures offer a way to assess that credit risk. The committee retains oversight, with borrower documents sent to it privately.

The framework posted by Kairos Research describes the delegated route; it identifies no newly funded borrower using it.

Eligibility combines bilateral lending with full recourse to the borrower or a parent guarantee, jurisdiction and standing checks, sanctions screening, segregated tri-party custody and verifiable daily collateral reports. The custody arrangement puts collateral with a third party.

Borrower documents go privately to the committee at least three business days before first funding. A member calling a borrower in must identify a failed criterion, with a decision within five business days. The committee therefore retains a criterion-based call-in power, while the text leaves funding during a pending objection unspecified. Cases outside the framework still require committee review.

The lending team sets loan-to-value rate cards, shared monthly with the committee alongside book reporting. Loan-to-value measures the amount lent relative to collateral value. A missed margin call, default or changed standing stops new lending to that counterparty and triggers escalation within 48 hours.

The permitted collateral includes BTC, ETH and other specified assets. USDe, sUSDe, ENA, USDtb and borrowers’ own assets are excluded; Ethereum-based XAUT is conditional on custody, liquidation venues and a weekend liquidation test.

The framework preserves five existing approvals. Its public text sets no numerical aggregate lending cap or per-borrower concentration ceiling.

Ethena’s institutional-lending documentation places aggregate exposure limits under governance and committee review, without publishing a numerical ceiling.

The earlier partner-by-partner approach is visible in the March 10 Maple and Anchorage proposal. It said additional offchain partners would need new proposals to the Risk Committee. LlamaRisk, Kairos Research and Blockworks Advisory subsequently posted support for the reviewed agreements.

Those reviews already kept contractual terms confidential. The October development concerns approval authority; confidentiality predates it.

The March discussion also shows why a partner’s brand is insufficient to identify the legal claim. Anchorage’s agreement was with A1, Ltd., a Cayman Islands group entity distinct from the OCC-chartered Anchorage Digital Bank. Maple’s pool-based agreement ran through Delaware’s Protocol Pool Operations Alpha LLC.

LlamaRisk’s April legal-review methodology names Ethena (BVI) Limited as the entity deploying reserve-backing assets. It examines collateral ratios, custody segregation, perfected security interests, liquidation rights, recall and redelivery timing, and practical enforceability.

The signing entity and contract matter because they govern the claim on borrowed assets and collateral.

Retaining the five existing approvals preserves arrangements with different structures; it does not establish that every legacy pool or warehouse meets the new bilateral criteria. LlamaRisk’s August 4 FalconX review described warehouse financing to FalconX International Lending Opportunities SPC for its segregated portfolio SP 1. The vehicle acquires institutional loan receivables and pledges its assets to Ethena, rather than operating as an ordinary bilateral borrower.

FalconX then announced a $1 billion secured SPV facility on August 19. That was an announced financing capacity, not a verified outstanding loan balance on November 1.

LlamaRisk’s October 12 assessment allocated 14.3% of backing to institutional lending, against total reported backing of roughly $5 billion and a similar USDe supply.

Blockworks Advisory’s Q3 governance update, published October 13, put institutional lending at 8% of liquid cash as of September 30. The two percentages use different dates and denominators. Comparing them as a rise from 8% to 14.3% would therefore be misleading.

CryptoSlate’s earlier lending analysis examined how allocations and recovery terms affect redemption liquidity. The October framework adds the question of which borrowers may enter through delegated screening.

Ethena’s lending dashboard describes collateral held with custodians, continuous LTV monitoring and no rehypothecation, meaning collateral is not pledged onward. In the November 1 text capture available for this report, borrower rows and the update time were unpopulated. It therefore provides no current borrower-level exposure or collateral ratios.

For holders, a usable assessment would put those elements together: the signing entity, its current outstanding amount, collateral and LTV, concentration against the relevant limit, and information to assess enforceability and recovery prospects.

#5188. What is EIP-7702? The Ethereum Upgrade Behind the $6.4B Binance Hack

What is EIP-7702? The Ethereum Upgrade Behind the $6.4B Binance Hack

Ethereum has two kinds of accounts. Externally owned accounts (EOAs), the standard wallets most people use, are controlled by a private key and can do little more than send transactions. Smart contract accounts can run code, enabling features like spending limits, account recovery and paying fees in tokens other than ETH, but most users never moved to them.

EIP-7702 is an attempt to bridge that gap. Instead of asking users to migrate to new wallets, it lets existing EOAs borrow the code of a smart contract while keeping their address and funds.

This guide explains what EIP-7702 is, how it works, why developers wanted it, and how attackers used it in the largest crypto theft on record.

EIP-7702, titled "Set Code for EOAs," is an Ethereum Improvement Proposal written in May 2024 by Ethereum co-founder Vitalik Buterin and developers Sam Wilson, Ansgar Dietrichs and lightclient, according to the official specification.

It was one of the headline changes in Pectra, a major Ethereum upgrade that went live on May 7, 2025.

"EIP-7702 lays the foundation we've needed for a long time," Ethereum core developer Preston Van Loon told Decrypt at the time. "While full abstraction won't be immediately available to end users with Pectra, this upgrade gives Ethereum the infrastructure to support it moving forward."

EIP-7702 is a change to Ethereum's core protocol, not a token or a separate blockchain, so there is nothing to buy.

EIP-7702 adds a new type of Ethereum transaction, the "set code transaction." It carries a list of authorizations, each signed with the private key of the account being upgraded, that names the address of a smart contract the account wants to use.

Once the transaction is processed, the account stores a small pointer, called a delegation indicator, to that contract. From then on, whenever anyone calls the account, Ethereum runs the contract's code as if it belonged to the account, with full access to its funds.

The delegation lasts until the account owner signs a new authorization, either pointing to a different contract or clearing the pointer to restore a plain EOA.

EIP-7702 marks delegated accounts using the byte 0xef, a code value that was banned in earlier upgrades so it could be reserved for special purposes like this.

The specification names three main features it was designed to deliver:

Those same features carry risks. Because delegated code gets "unrestricted access to the account," apps should never ask users to sign an EIP-7702 authorization, and wallets should not offer an interface for it, the specification warns. "There is no safe way to provide this interface," it says.

Scammers moved quickly. Within weeks of Pectra's launch, trading firm Wintermute found that more than 80% of EIP-7702 delegations pointed to copies of the same malicious "sweeper" contract, which automatically drains wallets whose keys have leaked, The Block reported in June 2025.

On October 29, 2026, an attacker moved some $6.4 billion out of Binance wallets on 10 blockchains, most of it within about 20 minutes. Blockchain analytics firms TRM Labs and Chainalysis both attributed the theft to TraderTraitor, the North Korean hacking group behind the $1.5 billion Bybit hack in February 2025.

On Ethereum, the attacker used an EIP-7702 delegation to turn a Binance hot wallet into a sweeper it controlled, with 50 authorizations signed in advance, according to TRM Labs. An operational wallet funded through crypto mixer Tornado Cash submitted the signed authorizations, which let the hot wallet run attacker-deployed code, and the attacker then called the wallet to sweep its assets into addresses it controlled, Chainalysis said.

Crucially, EIP-7702 delegations require a valid signature from the account being upgraded. Binance wallets on the other nine chains also sent funds to the attacker through ordinary signed transfers, which TRM said points to a compromise of Binance's hot wallet signing infrastructure rather than a flaw in Ethereum itself.

Binance has said its investigation began with a compromised engineer's laptop, and has since found "additional points of breach."

EIP-7702 is a first step toward full account abstraction, a long-standing goal of making every Ethereum account as flexible as a smart contract. Its authors designed it to be forward-compatible with that "endgame," so wallets built on it today should keep working as Ethereum's account model evolves.

The next step could come with Hegotá, the upgrade planned to follow Glamsterdam on Ethereum's roadmap. In February 2026, Buterin said that EIP-8141, which he called "an omnibus that wraps up and solves every remaining problem" account abstraction was meant to address, looked "possible to make happen within a year" in the Hegotá fork.

We have been talking about account abstraction ever since early 2016, see the original EIP-86: https://t.co/HYLSTLHgWH

Now, we finally have EIP-8141 ( https://t.co/jYqeS55j6P ), an omnibus that wraps up and solves every remaining problem that AA was…

— vitalik.eth (@VitalikButerin) February 28, 2026

"We have been talking about account abstraction ever since early 2016," Buterin wrote, adding that developers are also discussing whether existing accounts could join the new framework, which would give them batching and sponsored transactions natively.

It is part of a wider rebuild. In July 2026, Buterin described a new draft of Ethereum's long-term roadmap as the network's biggest overhaul since the Merge, its 2022 switch to proof of stake.

#5186. Aave Horizon taps perpetual futures to price stock collateral on weekends, Chainlink says

The moving reference can reveal off-hours collateral declines without ensuring a buyer for seized assets.

Chainlink says Aave's institutional lending platform Horizon is using its new weekend pricing service, giving tokenized stock collateral a reference that keeps moving while traditional exchanges are closed. The service was announced Oct. 29 and starts with high-volume U.S. equities, according to Chainlink's announcement.

The off-hours price comes from derivatives markets. During regular, extended and overnight sessions, Chainlink 24/7 uses its existing weekday data. When the underlying market closes, it draws quotes from multiple perpetual-futures venues, where derivatives linked to the asset continue trading. That reference reflects those markets' prices, rather than transactions on a closed stock exchange.

Chainlink says it filters stale inputs, limits outlier influence and joins the different sessions into one continuous series. Lending protocols receive the prices through onchain Data Feeds.

For a borrower, the consequence depends on how the feed enters collateral calculations. Where the moving reference is integrated into the market's risk rules, a weekend decline can reduce the value supporting a loan before the underlying exchange reopens. A position could cross its liquidation threshold during that window, rather than waiting for the reopening price update.

Horizon is a separate institutional instance of Aave. Only participants qualified and allowlisted by the collateral's issuer can supply its tokenized real-world assets and borrow against them. Its stablecoin pools, including USDC, RLUSD and GHO, accept liquidity from unrestricted suppliers. Collateral requirements and liquidation thresholds depend on the underlying asset.

Thanks for signing up to The Catalyst. Look out for your first daily email. If you already receive it, you’re all set.

For those stablecoin lenders, a moving valuation can make deteriorating collateral visible sooner. Debt recovery still depends on what can be realized from the collateral; an oracle reference by itself supplies neither a buyer nor redemption access.

Chainlink's announcement and Aave's Horizon documentation do not disclose the initial equity tokens, their feed addresses or equity-specific liquidation settings. Horizon's documentation describes bounds on issuer-reported net asset values that reject out-of-range updates and retain the last valid price. How those safeguards apply to the newly announced equity feeds is not specified in those pages.

This is distinct from Aave's V4 Equities Hub on Base, the subject of CryptoSlate's September weekend-gap coverage. Aave's Sept. 25 description of that market listed seven Coinbase stock tokens and feeds that held their last value over weekends. The Horizon announcement does not establish that Base's configuration has changed.

#5185. Elon Musk, CZ Trade Blows Over SpaceX Shares After $6.4B Binance Hack

Elon Musk, CZ Trade Blows Over SpaceX Shares After $6.4B Binance Hack

Binance co-founder Changpeng Zhao, better known as CZ, called out Elon Musk on Friday after the billionaire appeared to mock the exchange following a $6.4 billion hack—the largest in history—reminding him that Binance holds SpaceX shares worth more than $1 billion.

"Elon, have a lot of respect for you, but bad time to troll. Please stop," CZ wrote on X. "Our SPCX shares (from our $500m investment in your Twitter takeover) are worth more than a billion. Would appreciate if you show a little support. Thanks!"

The exchange followed Musk's response to a post estimating that, as a lot of the stolen funds have been frozen, Binance's losses could reach around $1 billion, despite holding approximately $144 billion in assets under custody.

Musk had replied to the discussion with a grimacing emoji, prompting another user to share an old post from former FTX CEO Sam Bankman-Fried claiming his exchange had enough money to cover its losses. FTX famously collapsed weeks later.

Elon, have a lot of respect for you, but bad time to troll. Please stop.

Our SPCX shares (from our $500m investment in your Twitter takeover) are worth more than a billion.

Would appreciate if you show a little support. Thanks!

— CZ 🔶 BNB (@cz_binance) October 30, 2026

CZ had initially responded to the loss estimate by saying the damage might be somewhat higher but that Binance would still be able to cover it. Musk's emoji appeared to draw an uncomfortable parallel with the FTX episode, although he did not elaborate on what he meant.

Of course, CZ didn't imply anything else about what Binance may do with its SpaceX shares.

Binance co-CEO Yi He also joined the exchange, addressing CZ in a post that appeared to respond to the suggestion that the company might need to liquidate its SpaceX holdings.

"Reporting to the major shareholder: Our company's revenue for the year is sufficient to cover the losses, so we do not yet need to sell the SpaceX shares you are bullish on," she wrote, adding a shrugging emoji.

CZ subsequently clarified that he had never suggested selling the shares. He said he was simply showing Musk some of the funds Binance had available, hoping to reassure him about the exchange's financial position.

😂 did say anything about selling SPCX shares, was just saying/showing some funds that he might know, so that he can be less worried.

— CZ 🔶 BNB (@cz_binance) October 30, 2026

The argument comes as Binance deals with the largest cryptocurrency theft on record. At least $6.4 billion in crypto was taken from wallets associated with the exchange on Thursday, October 29, across multiple blockchains.

Just yesterday, initial reports showed that at least $5.57 billion had left Binance-linked wallets across Ethereum, Bitcoin, and Tron. The attackers moved funds through several transactions, including a transfer of more than $1.6 billion in USDT to a newly created Ethereum address.

The incident also exposed an important weakness in how cryptocurrency can be recovered after a theft. Tether and Circle, the companies behind the USDT and USDC stablecoins, froze approximately $1.8 billion in assets associated with the attackers in the initial response, then subsequently froze a total of $2.39 billion. Unlike those tokens, Ethereum cannot be frozen by a central issuer, making it harder to stop stolen funds once they are converted.

The attackers subsequently used decentralized exchanges such as Uniswap and lending protocol Aave to move funds into Ethereum. These platforms allow users to trade or borrow cryptocurrency without relying on a traditional intermediary to approve each transaction. Decrypt tracked wallets receiving tens of thousands of ETH through these operations, including 19,762 ETH borrowed against approximately $60 million in USDT.

The suspected attackers have also been linked to North Korea. In a Friday report, blockchain analytics firms TRM Labs and Chainalysis attributed the attack to TraderTraitor, a North Korean hacking group previously blamed for the $1.5 billion Bybit hack in February 2025. Chainalysis estimated the Binance theft at approximately $6.4 billion.

Binance has since said it has reserved more than $6.34 billion in company funds to cover the incident, including $5 billion already held in the same tokens that were stolen. The exchange said it is acquiring the remaining assets through market and over-the-counter purchases, which can take longer when certain tokens have limited liquidity.

#5182. Solana study finds wider spreads outside aggregators at five of six sampled pools

The historical SOL/USDC comparison matched opposite-side fills within the same pools; wallet fees and current net execution costs are separate measures.

A September study found wider realized round-trip spreads for Solana trades routed outside aggregators at five of six major proprietary liquidity venues, suggesting that access to the same pool did not mean access to comparable trading terms.

The result puts the route into the center of the price comparison. A pool can settle trades on a public blockchain while a single operator controls its quotes. Comparing pool addresses alone therefore leaves out an important part of the transaction: how the order arrived.

The preprint by Ozan Solmaz, Lioba Heimbach and Jason Milionis, posted Sept. 29, examined proprietary automated market makers, or propAMMs, on Solana, Base and Monad. Its observations run from Sept. 1, 2025, through Aug. 31, 2026, with later starts for newer deployments. The Solana finding is a historical association between routing class and realized spreads, rather than a measurement of what every interface delivers today.

An aggregator searches across liquidity venues and can split an order among them. Jupiter is one such routing provider on Solana. A propAMM is the venue supplying liquidity: one operator provides inventory and can update prices directly, without waiting for a trade to change the pool's reserves.

The study compared aggregator and non-aggregator flow within the six largest measured Solana propAMMs for SOL/USDC. To estimate a realized round-trip spread, it paired consecutive fills on opposite sides of the same pool, within 25 slots and with trade sizes within a factor of two. There could be no intervening quote update from the operator, and both legs had to belong to the same routing class.

The researchers then compared the classes within cells defined by pool, day and trade-size bucket. That makes the test more informative than comparing unrelated venues or differently sized orders. It still pairs observed trades; the two fills need not be one customer's actual buy-and-sell journey.

Source: the study's Table 1. All spread figures are realized round-trip measurements. The matched premium is the median within-cell difference; the final column counts matched cells, not trades or trading volume.

One basis point is 0.01 percentage point. The distinction between the columns matters. Tessera's overall medians were 0.9 and 0.93 basis points, while its median matched premium was 0.1 basis points. The matched result comes from comparing the two classes inside each cell before summarizing those differences. Subtracting the displayed overall medians would answer a different question.

For the first five venues, non-aggregator spreads were wider in 68% to 83% of matched cells, with reported exact sign-test p-values below 0.001. ZeroFi went the other way on the matched median, with a small negative premium.

These are round-trip price gaps, not individual swap fees. Nor does the classification identify ordinary retail customers: non-aggregator flow can have different characteristics from aggregator flow. Matching pool, day and size reduces some differences between the groups, but it does not turn the comparison into a randomized test of what happens when the same person changes interfaces.

The economic rationale starts with stale prices. A passive pool can lose money when another trader acts on a market move before the pool's price catches up. PropAMM operators can send price updates without executing a swap, reducing that exposure when their updates arrive before incoming trades.

Start with the stories that matter. Get the context behind the headlines and a clear view of what to watch next.

Thanks for signing up to The Catalyst. Look out for your first daily email. If you already receive it, you’re all set.

That ability can benefit the operator through fewer stale-price losses. In the Solana sample, aggregator flow was also associated with tighter matched spreads at most of the measured venues.

The paper offers a more direct mechanism example on Base. In the Tessera implementation it decoded there, the researchers identified six fee components: base, age, priority, mode, default and address. The transaction sender and the contract calling Tessera's interface can affect default and address treatment, including exemptions and penalties.

That demonstrates how a proprietary venue can make caller identity part of execution pricing. It is evidence about the inspected Base implementation, rather than proof that Tessera's Solana deployment, or every other Solana propAMM, uses the same code or fee rules.

A separate issue is whether an attractive quote survives until settlement. The researchers documented Base Tessera fee decreases near block ends and increases near block starts, helping explain differences between the previous block's quote and the eventual fill. They explicitly lacked historical Solana quote data to corroborate an analogous Solana quote-to-fill gap.

Aggregator 0x's March investigation also reported Base quote-to-fill discrepancies involving Flashblock timing and spread changes. Its findings belong to that chain and to an interested routing provider's investigation.

A wallet charge can affect the amount a trader receives even when the underlying pool offers competitive terms. Phantom's current swap disclosure, checked Nov. 2, lists a 0.85% fee for most swaps, with exceptions, alongside variable network fees. The exceptions include specified Cash-account flows and deposits to a perps balance.

That wallet fee is a different measurement from the paper's round-trip spread. Price impact describes how an order's own size affects its price. Slippage describes the movement between a quote and execution. A slippage tolerance sets how much deterioration a transaction permits; raising it does not create a fixed fee, but it allows a worse fill.

Jupiter's current API documentation shows why the interface label also leaves questions unanswered. Its managed order path lets Metis, JupiterZ, Dflow and OKX compete, while its customizable build path uses Metis only. JupiterZ supplies request-for-quote liquidity, a separate mechanism from an on-chain propAMM pool.

Jupiter documents a 2-basis-point platform fee for SOL-to-stablecoin swaps on the managed order path, with other schedules for other categories. It lists no Jupiter swap fee on the build path, while integrator charges and network costs remain separate considerations.

Those disclosures describe available integrations and charges. They do not identify the route used by a particular Phantom swap or establish that a wallet avoids aggregators.

Adding a historical matched spread premium to a current wallet fee would therefore produce a misleading savings estimate. The comparison would mix a paired round-trip statistic with a charge on a particular swap, without establishing which costs were incremental or already reflected in the delivered amount.

Jupiter describes two approaches to reducing the quote-to-fill gap. Its execution-estimation account says routing incorporates observed execution data and simulation. Its JIT Swap description says candidate pools are carried into the transaction and re-quoted on-chain, allowing the selected pools or split to change when another candidate allocation offers a better output.

The useful test is therefore the completed trade: the route and venue used, the relevant charges, and the amount actually delivered under comparable conditions.

#5180. Binance Sets Aside $6.34B to Cover Hack Losses, CEO Says 'Your Assets Are Safe'

Binance Sets Aside $6.34B to Cover Hack Losses, CEO Says 'Your Assets Are Safe'

Binance will cover every dollar lost in this week's hack with its own money, CEO Richard Teng said in a video address on Friday, adding that customer funds were never touched.

"We are committed to covering all hacked losses through our company funds. Your assets are safe," Teng said, according to a transcript he tweeted.

An update on the security incident. pic.twitter.com/qn2804fO5z

— Richard Teng (@_RichardTeng) October 30, 2026

Binance has reserved more than $6.34 billion of company funds to cover the stolen assets, $5 billion of which is already held in the exact tokens taken, including USDC, USDT, BTC, ETH and BNB, he said. The exchange is buying the rest through market and over-the-counter channels.

Teng said the hack hit Binance's own hot wallet, not customer funds. "We never use any customer funds, so they are not impacted," he said, adding that most user assets are held in cold wallets and only moved into a hot wallet when a user withdraws. Binance's ability to absorb a loss of this size comes from keeping much of its operating earnings in crypto, he said.

Binance had earlier said it would cover all losses from its SAFU emergency fund and company funds.

Teng said the hack "was not carried out by one person." Binance initially traced it to a compromised laptop but has since found additional points of breach. The attacker also used a self-destruct mechanism that destroyed tooling and traces along the way, making the attack harder to investigate, Teng said.

The attacker used EIP-7702, an Ethereum feature that lets standard wallets run smart contract code, to obtain onchain execution approval over a hot wallet, according to Teng. "That delegation did not pass through our normal approval controls," he said, which is why Binance paused withdrawals to rebuild and retest its infrastructure. Blockchain analytics firms TRM Labs and Chainalysis have attributed the theft to North Korean hacking group TraderTraitor.

Binance hopes to restore withdrawals before its Sunday deadline, Teng said, and is carrying out a full security review covering endpoints, cloud, key management and signing. It plans to publish a full proof of reserves report by Monday, with an initial view before then.

The attacker is still moving funds. Between 11:37 a.m. and 12:12 p.m. ET on Friday, a Tron wallet that Arkham attributes to the hacker sent about 5.19 million TRX, worth roughly $1.73 million, to 12 addresses, according to Arkham data.

All markets are now unfrozen across Aave V3 and V4.

They were frozen as a precaution after initial reports, which paused new deposits and borrows while withdrawals and repayments continued as normal.

Decentralized lending protocol Aave, where the attacker borrowed ETH against USDT while racing stablecoin freezes, according to TRM Labs, said on Friday that it had unfrozen all markets on its V3 and V4 deployments. It had frozen them as a precaution after the first reports of the hack, pausing new deposits and borrowing while withdrawals and repayments continued. "Aave was unaffected, and all positions remain overcollateralized," founder Stani Kulechov wrote.

Binance co-founder Changpeng "CZ" Zhao said that while the $6.3 billion theft is the largest in dollar terms, it is not the largest in Bitcoin terms, pointing to Mt. Gox's loss of 650,000 BTC, more than 5% of the supply. Unlike at Mt. Gox and FTX, where nearly all users were affected, Binance is covering the losses itself, he said. "It hurts, but we survive," Zhao wrote.

#5176. Secret Service, FBI Join Binance Hack Probe as Circle Freezes $823 Million

Secret Service, FBI Join Binance Hack Probe as Circle Freezes $823 Million

Binance is working with the FBI and U.S. Secret Service after hackers stole $6.4 billion from the cryptocurrency exchange, as Circle joins the recovery effort by freezing more than $800 million in assets connected to the attack, according to Reuters.

A person familiar with the matter told Reuters that Binance was cooperating with law enforcement agencies around the world, including both U.S. agencies. The FBI confirmed it was aware of the incident and was working with public and private partners to assess its scope and coordinate investigative steps. The Secret Service remained silent.

Investigating financial crimes is part of the Secret Service’s job, beyond its better-known responsibility of protecting the president. The agency also investigates computer crimes, unauthorized access to computer systems, and crimes involving digital currencies.

Circle, the company behind USDC, a stablecoin designed to maintain a value of $1, has separately confirmed that it is coordinating with law enforcement. CEO Jeremy Allaire said the company had frozen more than $800 million in USDC associated with the Binance incident and would continue supporting the investigation.

Tether, which issues the competing USDT stablecoin, froze another $1.5 billion in tokens. Both companies can blacklist specific blockchain addresses, preventing the tokens they issue from being transferred out of those wallets. That ability gives stablecoin companies an unusual role in crypto investigations: they can effectively immobilize stolen money before investigators identify the people responsible.

Tether froze more than 1.567B USDT related to a potential Binance exchange hack.

Protecting the industry and our partners is our main priority.

— Paolo Ardoino 🤖 (@paoloardoino) October 29, 2026

This is not the first time the Secret Service has worked alongside crypto companies to investigate digital asset crimes. In April, Operation Atlantic—a joint effort involving the Secret Service, the U.K.'s National Crime Agency, Binance, Coinbase, and other crypto firms— identified more than 20,000 victims of approval phishing scams, traced $45 million in stolen funds and froze $12 million.

The Binance investigation also comes against the backdrop of a growing number of attacks attributed to North Korea. Blockchain intelligence firms TRM Labs and Chainalysis have both linked the $6.3 billion theft to TraderTraitor, a hacking group associated with the country's cyber operations. The same group has been linked to the $1.5 billion Bybit hack in February 2025, which the FBI also attributed to North Korean hackers.

North Korean attackers have targeted Binance itself before, including through attempts to infiltrate the exchange by posing as job applicants. In an interview with Decrypt in 2025, Binance Chief Security Officer Jimmy Su said North Korean operatives were contacting employees on LinkedIn every day, sometimes using fake interviews to deliver malware to their devices.

Binance has said it will cover all losses using company funds and its Secure Asset Fund for Users, an emergency reserve designed to protect customers in the event of a security breach. CEO Richard Teng said on Friday that the company had reserved more than $6.34 billion to cover the stolen assets, while approximately $3 billion had already been located or frozen with the help of industry partners.

The exchange initially suspected that a compromised laptop belonging to a DevOps engineer had provided the attackers with access to its systems. It has since identified additional points of breach and said it found no evidence that wallet private keys had been leaked. Withdrawals remain suspended while Binance reviews its security infrastructure, with the company targeting a resumption by Sunday.

#5172. Trezor reports suspected hardware implant in Safe 5 sold by CryptoBilis

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

Trezor has not received the device but is treating the risk as credible, urging customers who used units sold by CryptoBilis since March 2026 to move their funds.

Trezor urged customers who purchased hardware wallets from reseller CryptoBilis since March 2026 to migrate their funds immediately, following a responsible disclosure alleging a physical hardware implant in a Safe 5 device.

The Czech hardware wallet maker removed CryptoBilis from its authorized reseller network earlier this month following reports of tampered Ledger devices sold through the same distributor.

In a blog post published Friday, Trezor said it received the disclosure on Oct. 28 regarding a unit bought in August 2026 through Tokopedia. The company has not received the device for in-depth examination but it interviewed the customer by video call and is treating the report as credible, it added.

"We'd rather warn people now than wait for confirmation and find out someone lost their funds in the meantime," Trezor CCO Danny Sanders said in a statement to The Block. "If you bought a Trezor from CryptoBilis since March, move your funds to a new wallet and contact our Support team, and we'll send you a new device."

According to the post, the reported implant consists of an additional circuit board fitted between the device's main board and screen. It contains a cellular module and antenna that can reportedly read information displayed on the screen, including a wallet's recovery phrase, and transmit it over a mobile network.

Trezor said it began inspecting devices purchased from the reseller after removing it from its network. The company has received four customer devices for examination so far and said it found no signs of tampering in those units.

Rival wallet maker Ledger previously confirmed physical tampering in signers sold through CryptoBilis, while OneKey similarly severed ties with the reseller, froze new approvals, and offered free replacements.

For Trezor's part, no user funds loss has been reported. Onchain analysts estimated losses tied to the Ledger incident at more than $86 million across Bitcoin, Ethereum, and Tron, but the wallet maker has not confirmed those figures.

Meanwhile, the CryptoBilis incident is not the first security headache for Trezor this year. In August, a breach at shipping provider ShipMonk exposed personal data belonging to about 13,700 customers.

In September, Trezor disclosed that another approximately 67,000 U.S. customers were affected by the same breach. Also in the same month, the company said a breach at its third-party email provider had enabled phishing messages to be sent from Trezor's official domain.

#5170. $8 Million Crypto Kidnapping Lands Texas Brothers 15-Year Prison Terms

$8 Million Crypto Kidnapping Lands Texas Brothers 15-Year Prison Terms

Two Texas brothers have been sentenced to a combined 326 months in prison for a violent home-invasion robbery in which they held a Minnesota family at gunpoint for hours and forced the father to hand over more than $8 million in cryptocurrency.

Raymond Christian Garcia and Isiah Angelo Garcia, both of Waller, Texas, were sentenced on Thursday to 180 months and 186 months respectively, or 15 and 15-and-a-half years, by U.S. District Judge Donovan Frank in St. Paul, according to the Justice Department. Both had pleaded guilty in June.

According to court documents, the brothers traveled from Texas to Grant, Minnesota, and on Sept. 19, 2025, broke into the victim’s home, zip-tied the family and held them at gunpoint for more than nine hours while demanding access to the father’s crypto accounts.

When he failed to transfer the amount they expected, they forced him to drive one of the brothers to the family’s cabin in upstate Minnesota to retrieve additional hardware wallets and move the funds. In total, they compelled him to transfer over $8 million. The pair fled after the victim’s son called 911, and were later arrested near Houston.

“The Garcia brothers committed a brazen and calculated act of violence against the victim and his family,” U.S. Attorney Daniel Rosen said in a statement. The FBI’s Christopher Dotson said the brothers “violated the sanctity of a home” in “a brazen act of violence and greed.”

The case is a stark example of a “wrench attack,” in which criminals use physical force rather than hacking to seize crypto.

Such attacks have surged alongside crypto’s rising prices: Chainalysis counted 46 violent incidents in the first half of 2026 with more than $30 million stolen, while CertiK pegged losses even higher. Recent cases span a French kidnapping in which workers’ children were held hostage and a UK home invasion that ended in prison terms for the attackers.

The sentencing offers one of the clearest signals yet that U.S. courts are treating crypto wrench attacks as serious violent crime.

#5168. Coinbase Q3 net income jumps 66% as prediction markets gain traction

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

Bernstein analysts said Coinbase's expanding prediction markets and derivatives businesses helped offset weaker spot trading activity.

Coinbase Coinbase Global, Inc. (COIN) reported a sharp jump in third-quarter net income to $719 million, as its expanding prediction markets and derivatives businesses helped offset weaker spot trading activity, analysts said.

According to the exchange's earnings report released Thursday, the company generated $1.22 billion in total revenue, broadly flat from the previous quarter but down 35% year over year.

The company's net income rose 66% year on year to $719 million from $433 million in the same period last year. The results included a $715 million gain on crypto assets held for investment, compared with a $424 million gain in the year-ago quarter.

The exchange's spot trading volume fell 8% quarter over quarter to $135 billion, though the platform continued to gain market share, according to the report.

Notably, Coinbase's prediction markets business surpassed $200 million in annualized revenue, doubling from roughly $100 million in the previous quarter.

"Prediction markets have been one of the fastest-growing products in Coinbase history, growing even through slower sports months driven by crypto contracts where we have a strong right to win," Coinbase CEO Brian Armstrong said in a Thursday statement.

Bernstein analysts wrote in a Friday research note that the growth in prediction markets and subscription and services revenue "helped offset the softer crypto trading activity."

The analysts estimated that prediction markets and derivatives accounted for about 25% of the company's transaction revenue, equivalent to roughly 12% of its total revenue.

Coinbase's derivatives trading volume declined 7% quarter on quarter to roughly $986 billion, while the industry derivatives volumes declined by 11% quarter on quarter, according to Bernstein.

"Subscription and services revenue grew quarter over quarter and now represents nearly half of net revenue. Prediction markets are adding another revenue stream and are less correlated to crypto volatility," said Coinbase CFO Alesia Haas. "These businesses make Coinbase more diversified and resilient across market cycles."

Meanwhile, stablecoin-related revenue remained a key contributor to Coinbase's results. Its stablecoin revenue came in at $328 million, up 3% quarter on quarter. That figure included $21 million attributable to USDC held in corporate balances.

William Blair analysts said Thursday that stablecoins, particularly USDC, will revolutionize cross-border payments and "play a critical role in tokenized RWA settlement."

Both Bernstein and William Blair reiterated their Outperform ratings on Coinbase following the earnings release. Bernstein maintained its $250 price target on Coinbase's stock, while William Blair's valuation analysis suggested the stock could be worth roughly $240.

Coinbase's Nasdaq-listed shares closed down 1.07% at $160.59 on Thursday.

#5166. Strategy (MSTR) Adds 811 Bitcoin, Strive (ASST) Follows Suit With 124 as Both Keep Stacking

Strategy bought 811 bitcoin for $65.5 million and Strive added 124 BTC last week, with the two firms together adding 935 coins and Strategy now holding 849,327 BTC.

Strategy bought another 811 bitcoin last week. The Saylor-led firm spent about $65.5 million at an average of $80,846 per coin between October 26 and November 1, according to its 8-K filing on Monday. That brings total holdings to 849,327 BTC, bought for roughly $64.1 billion at an average of $75,451.

The money didn’t come from new preferred stock this time. Strategy sold about 1.18 million shares of MSTR common stock for roughly $164 million, sent $65.5 million into bitcoin, and parked the remaining $98.5 million in USD cash. It also spent around $208 million buying back about 2.1 million shares of its STRC preferred.

Strategy spent much of the year leaning on preferred issuance to fund purchases, and the company is now using the buyback to tidy up its preferred stack while still adding coins.

Saylor teased the purchase Sunday with his usual tracker chart and a line that made the rounds on X: “We found room for even more orange.”

The buy landed a few days after Strategy reported its third-quarter results on Thursday. Net income hit $19.5 billion, up from $2.79 billion a year earlier, driven by a $20.9 billion operating gain tied to the bitcoin price rising during the quarter. That gain is a paper one under fair-value accounting, not cash. Saylor called it the start of the next bitcoin bull market and pointed to bitcoin trading about 21% above its 200-week moving average. The same release said CFO Andrew Kang will step down November 5, with COO Reza Virasteh taking over the role in January.

Strategy shareholders also recently approved a proposal allowing four preferred stocks — STRF, STRC, STRK, and STRD — to accrue dividends daily, including weekends and holidays, with 99.3% of votes cast in favor. The change was intended to improve liquidity, reduce reinvestment delays, and increase price stability, while keeping dividend rates and total obligations unchanged. The new payment schedule remained subject to board approval and regulatory filings, with implementation expected to begin in November 2026 for STRC and January 2027 for the other preferred stocks.

BREAKING: Public companies Strive and Strategy bought a combined 934 Bitcoin worth $72.6 million.Strategy: 810 BTCStrive: 124 BTC pic.twitter.com/ObXGCz7weH

Strive is still in the race for second place among public holders. The Matt Cole-run firm added 124 BTC between October 24 and 30 at about $80,526 each, lifting its stack to 43,124 BTC.

That’s a smaller buy than its previous week, when it added more than 6,000 coins, but Cole has been consistent about his approach. He has said the company is buying “hand over fist” when prices dip, and he has framed the funding model around perpetual preferred equity as a way to avoid forced selling.

Strive’s cash position slipped about $14 million to $490 million over the same stretch, and it still holds 505,000 shares of Strategy’s STRC.

Meanwhile, the biggest story in crypto last week wasn’t a buy. On October 29, Binance was hit by what it now estimates is a $6.3 billion security breach, one that started with a compromised DevOps laptop.

Blockchain analytics firms including Chainalysis and TRM Labs have linked the attack to North Korea’s TraderTraitor group, and the drain reportedly took about 20 minutes. Binance says it has frozen roughly $3 billion of the stolen funds and will cover losses from its SAFU fund and company reserves.

Bitcoin slid toward $76,000 on the news, and it fell about 3.6% over the week.

Strategy’s stock dropped about 9% last week and closed Friday at $130.51. The company still has about $18.6 billion of MSTR available under its ATM program, which means more buys are likely if the market cooperates.

Strive shares were down around 20% last week. At the time of writing, bitcoin is near $78,000.

#5163. Binance Hack: Why Bitcoin Didn't Crash Like It Did in 2018

Binance Hack: Why Bitcoin Didn't Crash Like It Did in 2018

Bitcoin is down about 4% a day after the biggest crypto hack on record. In March 2018, a failed attack on the same exchange knocked nearly 11% off its price in minutes, and Binance said at the time that no funds had been stolen.

Binance has confirmed yesterday's attack, and Chainalysis and TRM Labs put the theft at roughly $6.4 billion, more than four times the previous record hack: the $1.5 billion Bybit heist of February 2025. Bitcoin dipped to about $76,400 on Friday, around 6% below where it traded after Wednesday’s Federal Reserve decision.

More than $1.3 billion in leveraged bets was liquidated, meaning trades placed with borrowed money that exchanges close automatically when prices move against them. And yet Bitcoin is holding relatively steady, all things considered.

The 2018 incident was a phishing and theft attempt: attackers took over accounts, created API keys (credentials that let trading bots act on an account) and used them to pump the obscure coin Viacoin while 31 accounts that had pre-deposited the coin sold it. Bitcoin fell from $10,740 to $9,690 within minutes and kept dropping to about $6,600 by April 5, though the market was already in a bear run after peaking near $20,000 in December 2017.

To have a grasp of the situation, the entire crypto market was worth just over $400 billion in March 2018 and is worth roughly $2.6 trillion now.

A big reason for that, and why panic doesn't hit markets the same way anymore, is U.S. spot Bitcoin ETFs. Funds like BlackRock’s iShares Bitcoin Trust let people buy Bitcoin through an ordinary brokerage account, and they now hold roughly 1.3 million BTC, about 6% of the supply.

Their shareholders don’t keep coins on Binance, and sovereign wealth funds such as Mubadala and the Abu Dhabi Investment Council have been buying. BlackRock’s fund alone took in $136 million on Wednesday, per Farside Investors data.

These funds don’t tend to panic sell so easily, and those funds remain locked for long periods of time.

The theft began at 4:05 p.m. ET Thursday, after U.S. markets closed, so Thursday’s preliminary $17 million of ETF inflows predate it and Friday’s flow figures are the first real read on whether those holders are selling.

The attacker still holds more than 420,000 ETH and 18,270 BTC, about $2.4 billion that no stablecoin issuer can freeze. The amount doesn’t account for other tokens, which would increase the holdings to more than $3 billion.

TRM and Chainalysis tie the attack to the North Korean group behind Bybit, which after that heist converted about 84% of the stolen Ethereum into Bitcoin through Thorchain, a cross-chain swap network.

Binance says it has set aside more than $6.34 billion of company funds to cover every loss, and it is buying the missing tokens on the open market and over the counter, meaning in private deals off the exchange. About $3 billion is frozen, deposits are operating normally, and withdrawals are on track to resume in 45 hours.

Market sentiment was already soft before the theft and slid further: CoinMarketCap’s Fear and Greed index, a 0-to-100 gauge of market mood, dropped to 36 points from 43 (now in “fear” territory after being “neutral” yesterday), and Bitcoin sits about 38% below its $126,080 record. Back in 2018, the market was well in “extreme fear” territory at 12 points on the scale when the incident happened. Traders were already in panic mode, and the attack just worsened an already ugly situation.

Glassnode data show long-term holders underwater on about 4 million coins bought between $85,000 and $118,000, and HashKey analyst Tim Sun said before the hack that the market’s problem “is not panic selling, but rather insufficient new buying interest.”

So, taken all together, what it all boils down to is this: A more optimistic market, much more liquidity, active long-term investors, a clearer regulatory framework, a sense of safety thanks to that regulatory clarity, quick action by stablecoin providers, and a price trend in recovery likely all played a role in keeping Bitcoin safe from a global panic despite the record-breaking hack.

#5160. Binance says it has $6.34 billion ready to cover hack losses

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

Binance already holds $5 billion in affected tokens and is buying the remainder, while industry partners have frozen approximately $3 billion.

Binance has allocated $6.34 billion in its own funds and reserves to cover losses stemming from an earlier security breach, the exchange said in a statement on Friday.

The exchange was hacked on Thursday, with investigations and recovery efforts alongside law enforcement ongoing. It pegged the loss at $6.3 billion.

According to the statement, Binance is holding about $5 billion of the committed funds in the same digital assets that were affected. The exchange did not specify the tokens, but Blockaid, while flagging the incident earlier, said that it was investigating billions in outflows from Binance hot wallets across Ethereum, Arbitrum, and Avalanche.

The exchange is buying the remaining roughly $1.3 billion in tokens through market and over-the-counter channels. However, it cautioned that the process could take longer because of thin liquidity and settlement delays.

Meanwhile, the reserve does not include approximately $3 billion in assets that Binance says industry partners have frozen. Tether CEO Paolo Ardoino separately said the stablecoin giant froze over $1.57 billion in USDT, while Circle confirmed more than $800 million in USDC had been frozen.

Ripple "promptly" froze roughly $200 million in associated funds, co-CEO Yi He said. World Liberty Financial also worked with BitGo and other partners to freeze approximately $27 million in stablecoins and $2 million in its own tokens, according to an X post.

In an earlier statement on Friday, Binance co-CEO Richard Teng said the initial investigation pointed to a compromised DevOps engineer's laptop, which gave an attacker access to a service container used to submit unauthorized withdrawal transactions.

Teng noted that the incident involved a more complex process than simply exploiting one employee's permissions. Binance has found no evidence of a wallet private key leak.

"Your money is safe, and we're covering every loss," Teng wrote.

The exchange has also suspended withdrawals, but users can still deposit assets, the co-CEO said.

Blockchain intelligence firm TRM Labs said several elements of the attack resemble previous incidents linked to North Korean hackers, including last year's $1.5 billion Bybit breach. Binance has not attributed the incident to a particular group.

TD Cowen said the breach exposed risks in exchange custody and cybersecurity, but did not undermine the underlying utility of digital assets or blockchain networks.

#5156. Reddit Stock Plunges 30% as Google's AI Search Hits Traffic and User Growth

Reddit Stock Plunges 30% as Google's AI Search Hits Traffic and User Growth

Reddit shares plunged 30.44% to $90.15 on Friday after CEO Steve Huffman warned that Google's AI-powered search features are cutting referral traffic and could shrink the platform's user base.

The selloff came after the company reported strong third-quarter results but issued a weaker-than-expected revenue forecast for the current quarter.

Reddit built much of its business around people discovering its discussions through Google searches, and those visitors are increasingly finding answers without ever clicking through to the platform. That puts pressure on the advertising business that has helped turn Reddit into one of the internet's fastest-growing publicly traded companies.

Reddit's third-quarter numbers initially looked healthy. Revenue climbed 53% year over year to $895 million, advertising revenue increased 54% to $847 million, and net income reached $283 million. Diluted earnings per share, which measure company profit attributable to each share while accounting for potential dilution, came in at $1.40, up 75% from a year earlier and above the $1.33 analyst consensus.

But the audience figures were less encouraging since Reddit reported 130.3 million daily active unique visitors, up 12% year over year but below the roughly 134 million analysts expected. In the U.S., the number actually fell 2% to 50.7 million, giving investors another reason to question whether the platform can maintain its growth.

Huffman delivered the warning during Thursday's earnings call, explaining that Google search referrals had fallen by more than one million on average every week during October, with the decline accelerating toward the end of the third quarter. If that pace continues, Reddit's total daily active users could fall by a low double-digit percentage in Q4 from the current base of around 130 million.

Huffman pointed directly at Google's AI Overviews and AI Mode, features that generate answers and summaries directly inside search results. Rather than sending users to websites to find information, Google increasingly gives them what they are looking for on its own page, pushing traditional search links further down.

Asked whether the changes were responsible for Reddit's declining search traffic, Huffman said the AI features had expanded aggressively in the U.S. and internationally. "The AI mode does not drive traffic, not for us and not to anybody as far as I know," he told investors.

That creates an awkward situation for Reddit—and arguably every other single media site on the internet. People can still search for Reddit discussions, but Google's results increasingly allow them to consume information from those discussions without visiting Reddit itself. Search referrals also bring back existing users, meaning fewer clicks can affect both new-user acquisition and the platform's ability to keep people engaged.

Reddit is trying to reduce that dependence by moving toward a primarily logged-in experience, which would require people to sign into an account before accessing most of the platform. The company plans to begin testing the change in selected markets outside the U.S. during the fourth quarter.

The strategy also connects to Reddit's increasingly important relationship with the AI industry. In February 2024, Reddit signed a reported $60 million-a-year data licensing deal with Google, giving the search giant access to Reddit discussions to train AI models. A few months later, the company announced a similar partnership with OpenAI, allowing the ChatGPT developer to use Reddit content while giving Reddit access to OpenAI's technology.

Those deals turned Reddit's enormous archive of human conversations into something it could sell to AI developers. But the same AI-powered search experience that benefits from Reddit's content can reduce the number of people who actually visit the site. Reddit also started tightening access to its public data and moving toward a more restricted platform which angered long term users.

The stock has now lost 44% over the past week, with Friday's 30.44% plunge accounting for a significant portion of the decline. At $90.15, Reddit is trading well below the levels it held just days ago.

The technical picture offers little immediate relief. Reddit's 50-day exponential moving average, a trend indicator that gives greater weight to recent prices, sits around $149.80, while its 200-day average is near $162.12. The 50-day average has already fallen below the 200-day average, creating a so-called death cross, a bearish signal commonly associated with a longer-term downward trend.

At $90.15, Reddit would need to climb approximately 66%—almost 70%—just to return to its 50-day average. That would only bring the share price back to the shorter-term trend line, not undo the death cross itself. With both averages sitting far above the current price, a recovery to those levels in the near term looks increasingly unlikely.
